Per-deliverable spec at `artifacts/D-0117/spec.md`; evidence under `evidence/T06.15/`.\n20\t\n21\t---\n22\t\n23\t## D-1: PTY layer — fork ptytest vs build minimal pexpect wrapper\n24\t\n25\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n26\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n27\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** NFR-MAINT1 (roadmap row 23) — vendored ptytest fork under `cli/eval/pty/`; AC10 (row 25) — fork SHA pin + drift policy; COMP-007 (row 36) — PtyDriver consumes the vendored layer.\n28\t\n29\t### Context\n30\t\n31\tReal Claude Code is a TTY-based interactive REPL. The eval harness needs a PTY (pseudo-terminal) driver that can spawn it, write to its stdin, read from its stdout/stderr, detect \"prompt ready\" states, kill on timeout, and capture exit codes.\n32\t\n33\t### Options\n34\t\n35\t| Option | Pros | Cons | LOC | Risk |\n36\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n37\t| **A. Fork `brandon-fryslie/ptytest`** | Working Claude Code example already exists in repo. Plugin-free standalone class. Active enough (recent commits). | 1 star / 0 forks → bus-factor real. Includes pytest-fixture machinery we don't need. | ~300 LOC adaptation | LOW (ptytest is small, easy to own) |\n38\t| B. Build minimal `pexpect` wrapper from scratch | Clean ownership, no upstream-drift risk, exactly the API we want. | More code to write, debug, test. We re-derive Claude Code TTY quirks. | ~700 LOC fresh | MEDIUM (pexpect has subtle gotchas) |\n39\t| C. Use `pexpect` directly (no wrapper) | Zero abstraction overhead. | Every eval re-implements spawn/wait/expect_prompt patterns; code duplication. | ~0 LOC harness; +~50 LOC per eval | HIGH (drift across evals) |\n40\t\n41\t### Decision: **A — fork ptytest, vendor under `cli/eval/pty/`, fork-and-own posture.**\n42\t\n43\t### Rationale\n44\t\n45\t1. The fork-research report verified ptytest has a working Claude Code example, which means the hardest TTY edge cases (prompt detection, ANSI escape handling, line-buffered reads) are already solved.\n46\t2. Bus-factor risk is mitigated by **vendoring** (we copy the code in; no runtime pip dependency on the upstream repo). If upstream dies, we have a frozen working copy.\n47\t3. The durable dependency is `pexpect` (2.8k stars, active again at v4.9) — ptytest is just a thin layer on top.\n48\t4. 300 LOC adaptation is ~5x cheaper than 700 LOC fresh build.\n49\t5. Option C (raw pexpect) fails the harness-cleanliness goal — evals should look like assertion code, not PTY plumbing.\n50\t\n51\t### Consequences\n52\t\n53\t- We own a small vendored fork forever. `PROVENANCE.md` documents the fork SHA and our diffs.\n54\t- Quarterly review of upstream `pexpect` releases; resync if security-relevant.\n55\t- A new file at `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` reproducing upstream MIT terms (license compliance).\n56\t\n57\t---\n58\t\n59\t## D-2: Assertion DSL — adopt `Expect.*` port vs raw assertions\n60\t\n61\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n62\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n63\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** COMP-010 (roadmap row 12) — ExpectDSL interface in M1; FR-EXP1 (row 64) — Expect.* primitives in M4; COMP-010.1..COMP-010.6 (rows 65–70) — per-primitive contracts.\n64\t\n65\t### Context\n66\t\n67\tEach eval needs to verify hook side-effects (file presence, JSONL events, settings.json registrations, exit codes, TTY output). The expression of these assertions can be:\n68\t\n69\t### Options\n70\t\n71\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n72\t|---|---|---|\n73\t| **A. Port `mcp-eval`'s `Expect.tools.*` DSL idea as Python primitives** | Declarative YAML manifest possible. Reusable across evals. Single place to add new assertion types. Mental-model match with industry pattern. | +~200 LOC for the DSL itself. Some abstraction overhead (declarative → callable). |\n74\t| B. Raw Python `assert` statements in each eval | Lowest abstraction. No new code. | Each eval is more code. Declarative YAML manifest not possible (or only partial). Failure messages are ad-hoc per eval. |\n75\t| C. Use pytest's assertion machinery directly | Free; idiomatic. | Couples harness to pytest, but the harness is NOT pytest-driven (it's a CLI subcommand). |\n76\t\n77\t### Decision: **A — port `Expect.*` DSL, ~200 LOC, no upstream dependency.**\n78\t\n79\t### Rationale\n80\t\n81\t1. The YAML manifest schema (§5 of design-spec) is a hard requirement to keep eval definitions declarative and reviewable. Raw Python assertions can't be expressed in YAML.\n82\t2. A small DSL (~10 method types: `file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) covers all 15 evals comfortably.\n83\t3. The mcp-eval API surface is a proven mental model — we steal the ergonomics without taking a dependency on the framework (which has the wrong architecture for our needs).\n84\t4. Failure messages from a typed DSL are uniformly high-quality (the DSL knows its own structure); ad-hoc assertions tend to produce noisy error output.\n85\t5. The DSL has a programmatic escape hatch for parameterized evals that can't be expressed in YAML — so we don't lock ourselves out of complex assertions.\n86\t\n87\t### Consequences\n88\t\n89\t- `cli/eval/expect.py` is a public API surface; changes need backward-compat care once evals are written against it.\n90\t- The YAML schema (`suites/suite.schema.json`) and the DSL must stay in lockstep — `loader.py` translates declarative → callable.\n91\t- Out-of-scope for v1: LLM-judge assertions (mcp-eval has these; we don't need them for hook validation).\n92\t\n93\t---\n94\t\n95\t## D-3: HOME isolation — extend existing `IsolationLayers` vs separate class\n96\t\n97\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n98\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n99\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** FR-ISO1 (roadmap row 28) — HomeIsolation extends IsolationLayers; COMP-006 (row 32) — HomeIsolation implementation; FR-ISO2 (row 29) — path containment guard composed alongside.\n100\t\n101\t### Context\n102\t\n103\t`src/superclaude/cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182` already implements 4-layer per-subprocess isolation (`CLAUDE_WORK_DIR`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DIR`, `CLAUDE_SETTINGS_DIR`). The eval harness needs a 5th layer: `HOME` override (and `XDG_*` companions). Question: extend the existing class or build a new one?\n104\t\n105\t### Options\n106\t\n107\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n108\t|---|---|---|\n109\t| **A. Compose `IsolationLayers` in a new `HomeIsolation` class** | Reuses 4 existing layers for free. No risk to sprint's existing usage. New `HOME` layer lives in eval code, not sprint code. | Two classes to maintain. Slight indirection. |\n110\t| B. Add `HOME` layer to `IsolationLayers` directly (modify sprint code) | One unified class. Sprint also benefits from HOME isolation (theoretical future use). | Modifies code outside this release's scope. Sprint doesn't currently need HOME isolation, so YAGNI risk. Sprint regression risk. |\n111\t| C. Build `HomeIsolation` standalone (no inheritance/composition) | Maximum decoupling. | Duplicate 4 layers of code. Bug-fixes diverge over time. |\n112\t\n113\t### Decision: **A — `HomeIsolation` COMPOSES `IsolationLayers` (has-a, not is-a).**\n114\t\n115\t### Rationale\n116\t\n117\t1. Composition preserves sprint's existing class verbatim. Zero risk to PR #49-era infrastructure.\n118\t2. The `HOME` layer is conceptually a \"5th layer,\" but its lifecycle (mkdtemp → install hooks → seed state → teardown) is more complex than the existing 4 (which are just env-var settings). Keeping it separate cleanly captures that asymmetry.\n119\t3. If a future sprint feature ever needs HOME isolation, it can either re-use `HomeIsolation` directly or migrate the logic upward — easier path than splitting later.\n120\t4. The interface in `design-spec.md` §7 already reflects this: `HomeIsolation` exposes `.env()` that returns the dict overlay, which the caller merges with `IsolationLayers.env()` output.\n121\t\n122\t### Consequences\n123\t\n124\t- `HomeIsolation` constructor takes an `IsolationLayers` instance as a dependency.\n125\t- Documentation in `cli/eval/isolation.py` cross-references `cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182`.\n126\t- If the sprint `IsolationLayers` API ever changes shape, we have a single integration point to update.\n127\t\n128\t---\n129\t\n130\t## D-4: Eval registry — YAML manifest vs Python-decorator-registered\n131\t\n132\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n133\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n134\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** DM-011 (roadmap row 2) — Suite manifest YAML schema; DM-002 (row 3) — EvalSpec model; FR-SCH1 (row 4) — manifest schema validation; COMP-002 (row 6) — SuiteLoader.\n135\t\n136\t### Context\n137\t\n138\tThe 15 evals need to be defined somewhere. The orchestrator needs to enumerate them, validate dependencies, schedule them, and report results. Options diverge on where the \"source of truth\" lives.\n139\t\n140\t### Options\n141\t\n142\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n143\t|---|---|---|\n144\t| **A. YAML manifests under `suites/*.yaml`** | Declarative, human-readable, reviewable diffs, schema-validated, easy to add new suites (`fast.yaml`, `nightly.yaml`). Non-Python contributors can author/review evals. | Some Python required for complex pre/post logic; needs an \"escape hatch\" mechanism. |\n145\t| B. Python-decorator-registered (`@eval(id=\"E1\", title=\"...\", requires=[...])`) | Maximum flexibility — eval bodies are first-class Python. Type-checking friendly. | Diff churn higher (Python > YAML in PR review noise). Adding new suites requires more boilerplate. Schema enforcement is via type hints, less ergonomic for non-Python contributors. |\n146\t| C. Hybrid — YAML defines metadata, Python `@assertion_callback` for escape hatch | Best of both worlds for simple+complex evals. | More mechanism to learn; two source-of-truth surfaces. |\n147\t\n148\t### Decision: **A — YAML manifest as primary, with optional Python escape-hatch via `callback:` field for parameterized/complex evals.**\n149\t\n150\t### Rationale\n151\t\n152\t1. **Reviewability** is paramount: a PR adding a new eval should be readable as a YAML diff, not as a Python class with overrides. The 15-eval suite is meant to be a living spec — declarative beats imperative.\n153\t2. The schema (§5 of design-spec) covers the common case (file/JSONL/settings/exit/stderr/duration assertions) elegantly.\n154\t3. The `callback:` field provides escape-hatch power without forcing every eval through it:\n155\t   ```yaml\n156\t   - id: E14\n157\t     title: \"concurrent SessionStart bursts\"\n158\t     callback: superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start\n159\t   ```\n160\t   The callback is invoked only when an eval's logic can't be expressed in YAML (E14, possibly E15).\n161\t4. New suites (`fast.yaml`, `regression.yaml`, etc.) drop in as data files with no code changes — exactly the kind of low-friction extension we want.\n162\t5. `loader.py` validates against `suites/suite.schema.json` once; the rest of the pipeline trusts validated input.\n163\t\n164\t### Consequences\n165\t\n166\t- `suites/suite.schema.json` is a public artifact (committed) — schema changes need versioning.\n167\t- Callbacks live in `suites/<suite-name>_callbacks.py` co-located with the YAML.\n168\t- Eval IDs (`E1`, `E2.1`, etc.) are stable identifiers — must not be renamed without a migration note.\n169\t- Parameterized evals (e.g., E2's 3 prefix variants) use a `parameterize:` block in YAML; the loader expands these into separate `EvalSpec` instances at load time.\n170\t\n171\t---\n172\t\n173\t## D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate — falsifiable definition of \"equivalent regression\"\n174\t\n175\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n176\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n177\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** FR-G5 (roadmap row 75) — falsifiable hook-matcher coverage gate CLI entry; TEST-013 (row 101) — coverage-gate tests; MIG-002 (row 103) — eval-batch rollout references coverage map.\n178\t\n179\t### Context\n180\t\n181\tGoal G5 in design-spec §1 said \"catch the bug PR #49 fixed and any equivalent future regression\" — but \"equivalent\" was undefined. Spec-panel finding W-1 (CRITICAL) flagged this as unfalsifiable: with no measurable acceptance criterion, the harness has no way to answer \"did I do my job?\"\n182\t\n183\t### Options\n184\t\n185\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n186\t|---|---|---|\n187\t| **A. Coverage gate: every matcher P in `hooks.json` must have ≥1 eval in the loaded suite** | Falsifiable, mechanically enforceable. CI-friendly. Maps to the actual class of bugs PR #49 lived in (matcher↔case-body drift). | Requires `eval doctor --check-coverage`. New eval must accompany every new hook matcher. |\n188\t| B. Snapshot match — assert this commit's matchers equal a frozen golden list | Simple. | Frozen list goes stale; bug-class definition is \"matches a list\" not \"real-world hook fires.\" |\n189\t| C. Leave G5 prose-only (\"intent is clear enough\") | Zero work. | Spec-panel CRITICAL stands; downstream tasklist has no test for G5. |\n190\t\n191\t### Decision: **A — coverage gate enforced by loader + `eval doctor --check-coverage`.**\n192\t\n193\t### Rationale\n194\t\n195\t1. The class of bug PR #49 introduced was \"matcher exists in `hooks.json` but no real-world test exercises it.\" A coverage gate directly inverts that condition.\n196\t2. Mechanical enforcement at load time means the harness fails fast (exit 2) when a new matcher lands without an eval — no silent drift.\n197\t3. The gate runs in two places: (a) `eval doctor` for pre-flight, (b) top of `eval run` for the live run. Both are cheap (single pass over `hooks.json` + manifest).\n198\t\n199\t### Consequences\n200\t\n201\t- `loader.py` reads `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` at run-start, extracts every PostToolUse matcher pattern, and verifies ≥1 eval in the manifest declares `inputs.expect_tool_call` matching each pattern.\n202\t- New hook matcher PRs must include a paired eval — enforced by a CI lint (deferred per non-goals but the harness itself enforces locally).\n203\t- Acceptable escape hatch: manifest declares `coverage_exempt_matchers: [\"<pattern>\"]` with a `reason:` field for matchers intentionally untestable.\n204\t\n205\t---\n206\t\n207\t## D-6: Disk-budget enforcement strategy\n208\t\n209\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n210\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n211\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** NFR-PERF4 (roadmap row 60) — `--max-disk-mb` disk-budget enforcement at 5s polling; COMP-003 (row 57) — RunOrchestrator hosts DiskBudgetWatchdog sidecar.\n212\t\n213\t### Context\n214\t\n215\tSpec-panel finding N-2 (CRITICAL) flagged internal inconsistency: §14 R4 cited `--max-disk-mb` as the mitigation but §4 flag table omitted the flag. Either add it or strike the mitigation.\n216\t\n217\t### Options\n218\t\n219\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n220\t|---|---|---|\n221\t| **A. Add `--max-disk-mb` flag (default 1024), poll every 5s, abort-on-breach** | Closes the cited risk. Bounded blast radius. Simple implementation. | One more flag. Polling adds tiny CPU overhead. |\n222\t| B. Strike the mitigation from R4; document \"user must monitor disk manually\" | Zero code. | R4 becomes a known accepted risk; eval runs on a constrained box can fill disk. |\n223\t| C. Hard limit via `setrlimit(RLIMIT_FSIZE)` on each subprocess | Kernel enforced. | Per-process limit, not cumulative; doesn't catch the 15-parallel-evals case. |\n224\t\n225\t### Decision: **A — add `--max-disk-mb` flag with 5-second polling.**\n226\t\n227\t### Rationale\n228\t\n229\t1. The risk is real: 15 parallel evals × keep-home-on-failure × TTY transcripts can fill a small disk silently. A polling guard with graceful abort (in-flight evals complete, new ones blocked) preserves results-so-far.\n230\t2. 5-second polling is cheap: one `du -sb` (or Python `os.walk` accumulator) per tick. At 15-parallel, the orchestrator is mostly blocked on pexpect anyway.\n231\t3. Default of 1024 MB matches design-spec §12 sizing (~300 MB total artifacts at 15-eval max-keep). Generous but not unbounded.\n232\t\n233\t### Consequences\n234\t\n235\t- New flag `--max-disk-mb INT` in §4 flag table; default 1024; `0` disables.\n236\t- New orchestrator component `DiskBudgetWatchdog` running in a sidecar thread.\n237\t- New exit-2 reason code: `disk_budget_exceeded`.\n238\t- Summary.json gains `harness.peak_disk_mb` field (also satisfies spec-panel H-1 partial).\n239\t\n240\t---\n241\t\n242\t## D-7: Path-traversal hardening for HomeIsolation\n243\t\n244\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n245\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n246\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** FR-SCH2 (roadmap row 5) — eval_id regex guard; FR-ISO2 (row 29) — path containment guard; AC12 (row 16) — allowed scratch roots; NFR-SEC1 (row 7) — path-traversal negative-case tests.\n247\t\n248\t### Context\n249\t\n250\tSpec-panel finding Wh-1 (CRITICAL) demonstrated that a malformed `eval_id` (e.g., `../../../etc/passwd` or `{session_id}` self-reference) could cause `HomeIsolation` to write outside the scratch root. The §14 R7 \"Hard guard\" was prose-only and ambiguous about whether `..` traversal was resolved before the containment check.\n251\t\n252\t### Options\n253\t\n254\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n255\t|---|---|---|\n256\t| **A. Defense-in-depth: loader regex + HomeIsolation `Path.resolve().is_relative_to()` + symlink-resolution after creation + scratch_root allowlist** | Catches the attack at three independent layers. Fails closed at every layer. | More code (~30 LOC). Three checks to maintain. |\n257\t| B. Loader-only regex validation | Simple. | Single layer = single failure point. |\n258\t| C. Trust the manifest author (no validation) | Zero work. | Manifest is data; data ingestion without validation is the original sin of basically every security incident. |\n259\t\n260\t### Decision: **A — three-layer defense-in-depth (regex + Path containment + symlink resolution + allowlist).**\n261\t\n262\t### Rationale\n263\t\n264\t1. The harness operates on user-supplied YAML and writes to disk. This is the textbook injection surface. Single-layer validation is industry-known-insufficient.\n265\t2. Each layer catches a distinct attack class: regex blocks `..` and template tokens; `Path.resolve()` normalizes any survivor; `is_relative_to(scratch_root)` enforces containment; symlink resolution catches the \"scratch dir is a symlink to `$HOME`\" race; allowlist on `scratch_root` itself blocks `--output-dir /home/user/.claude`.\n266\t3. Python 3.10+ (project minimum) provides `Path.is_relative_to()` natively.\n267\t4. The performance cost is negligible (4 stat calls per eval setup).\n268\t\n269\t### Consequences\n270\t\n271\t- `HomeIsolation.setup()` gains a documented pre-flight sequence (regex re-check → resolve → contain → symlink-resolve → write).\n272\t- `EvalConfig` gains an `allowed_scratch_roots: tuple[Path, ...]` field, defaulting to `(/tmp/eval-runs, <repo>/.dev/eval-runs)`.\n273\t- New exception type `HomeContainmentViolation` (subclass of `EvalHarnessError`); maps to status ERRORED.\n274\t- Test `test_isolation.py` MUST include: (a) `..` traversal, (b) absolute-path eval_id, (c) symlink-to-home attack, (d) `--output-dir` outside allowlist.\n275\t\n276\t---\n277\t\n278\t## D-8: Reporter dimensional invariant (consumes N', not K)\n279\t\n280\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n281\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n282\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** COMP-008 (roadmap row 55) — Reporter / AggregatedRunReport; FR-RPT1 (row 54) — aggregated run report dimensional invariant; COMP-003 (row 57) — RunOrchestrator emits one EvalOutcome per expanded EvalSpec.\n283\t\n284\t### Context\n285\t\n286\tSpec-panel Pipeline-CRITICAL showed §9's summary.json example reported `14 passed, 1 failed, 0 skipped` (totals = 15, the full suite) but the spec text described capability filtering as removing evals from the schedule. If the reporter only sees the K-kept subset, a `--no-mcp` run that drops 5 evals would show \"10 passed\" with no audit trail of the 5 skipped — a silent regression in coverage measurement.\n287\t\n288\t### Options\n289\t\n290\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n291\t|---|---|---|\n292\t| **A. Orchestrator emits N' EvalOutcomes (one per expanded EvalSpec); SKIPPED carries reason; reporter asserts `len(outcomes) == N'`** | Audit trail preserved. Coverage queries answerable. Contract is checkable. | Slightly more state per skipped eval. |\n293\t| B. Reporter takes K outcomes + a separate skipped-list; merges at render time | Same audit trail. | Two channels to keep in sync. More places to drop a skip. |\n294\t| C. Status quo (K only, skip count is metadata) | Less code. | The spec-panel CRITICAL stands; this is the bug being filed. |\n295\t\n296\t### Decision: **A — single list of N' EvalOutcome records; reporter asserts dimensional invariant.**\n297\t\n298\t### Rationale\n299\t\n300\t1. A single dataclass list eliminates the \"two channels in sync\" failure mode of option B.\n301\t2. The contract assertion (`len(outcomes) == counts.expanded_n_prime`) makes the invariant grep-able and test-able; CI can verify it doesn't regress.\n302\t3. Status taxonomy expansion (8 statuses with explicit FAIL/ERRORED/TIMEOUT/INTERRUPTED/SKIPPED/XFAIL/XPASS distinction) is a natural consequence — addresses the spec-panel \"failure-mode taxonomy is sparse\" consensus point.\n303\t4. Performance cost is zero in practice (the SKIPPED records are empty-shell; N' is at most 17 with parameterize expansion).\n304\t\n305\t### Consequences\n306\t\n307\t- New dataclass `EvalOutcome` in `models.py` (see design-spec §9 \"Aggregator data contract\").\n308\t- New status values `ERRORED`, `INTERRUPTED`, `XFAIL`, `XPASS` (in addition to PASS/FAIL/TIMEOUT/SKIPPED) — taxonomy frozen in design-spec §9.\n309\t- New error `ReporterContractViolation` raised on dimensional mismatch at `AggregatedRunReport.from_outcomes()`.\n310\t- Summary.json gains `counts` block (manifest_n, expanded_n_prime, kept_k, skipped_s).\n311\t- Exit-code mapping in §4 cross-references this taxonomy.\n312\t\n313\t---\n314\t\n315\t## Cross-cutting decision: implementation order\n316\t\n317\t(Not a strict ADR, but worth recording.) Per §17 of `design-spec.md`, the implementation phases are:\n318\t\n319\t1. **Phase 1** (1 day): vendored `pty/`, `HomeIsolation`, capability gates, `eval doctor`. Smoke: `superclaude eval doctor` green.\n320\t2. **Phase 2** (1 day): `loader.py`, `models.py`, `expect.py`, `eval describe/list`. Smoke: parse a 1-eval manifest.\n321\t3. **Phase 3** (1 day): `orchestrator.py`, `runner.py`, `reporter.py`, `eval run`. Smoke: 1 real Claude Code session driven end-to-end.\n322\t4. **Phase 4** (0.5 day): wire into `cli/main.py`, Makefile target, .gitignore. Smoke: `make verify-sync` still EXIT=0.\n323\t5. **Phase 5** (1-2 weeks): implement E1-E15 in 3-5 PR batches.\n324\t\n325\t**Rationale for this ordering:** Phase 1 is the riskiest (PTY mechanics). Validating the harness can spawn-and-observe ONE Claude Code session is the gate that determines whether the entire approach is viable. If Phase 1 fails (e.g., Claude Code's TTY behavior breaks pexpect-style detection), we re-pivot before committing to the rest of the architecture.\n326\t\n327\t---\n328\t\n329\t## Sign-off\n330\t\n331\t| Decision | Status | Signed | Date |\n332\t|---|---|---|---|\n333\t| D-1: PTY layer = fork ptytest | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n334\t| D-2: Assertion DSL = Expect.* port | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n335\t| D-3: HOME isolation = compose IsolationLayers | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n336\t| D-4: Eval registry = YAML + callback escape | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n337\t| D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate (G5 falsifiable) | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n338\t| D-6: `--max-disk-mb` poller (R4 enforcement) | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n339\t| D-7: Three-layer path-traversal hardening | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n340\t| D-8: Reporter consumes N' + status taxonomy | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n341\t| D-10: NOTICE/LICENSE attribution for vendored ptytest | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n342\t\n343\t**Maintainer:** sign-off pass complete (R5, 2026-05-20). All 8 ADRs plus D-10 approved; SC1 acceptance criterion satisfied. Future revisions follow the \"Reject/revise\" rule below.\n344\t\n345\t**Reject/revise:** add a row to a \"Revisions\" section below; do not edit the original decisions in-place.\n346\t\n347\t---\n348\t\n349\t## Revisions\n350\t\n351\t(none yet)\n352\t\n353\t---\n354\t\n355\t## D-9: Reconcile `validation.status: \"fail\"` label and document manual-triage policy for unclassified deviations\n356\t\n357\t### Context\n358\t\n359\t`.roadmap-state.json:113-117` reports `fidelity_status: \"pass\"` and `validation.status: \"fail\"` (timestamp 2026-05-18T19:47:24Z, six minutes after the final pipeline step completed). However, every pipeline step in `steps.*` — including `spec-fidelity`, `wiring-verification`, `deviation-analysis`, and `remediate` — recorded `status: \"PASS\"`. `spec-fidelity.md:1-7` likewise records 0 HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW deviations and PASS convergence.\n360\t\n361\tThe `validation.status: \"fail\"` label was stamped by a separate validator step that reacts to the 20 records in `spec-deviations.md`. Per `spec-deviations.md:10`, \"deviation classification is not yet implemented. All records currently render as UNCLASSIFIED.\" `remediation-tasklist.md:14-34` then escalates all 20 to `BLOCKING` with owner `UNKNOWN` purely because the classifier never ran — not because spec-fidelity actually regressed.\n362\t\n363\tA manual cross-check (performed during this reconciliation) confirms:\n364\t\n365\t- All 15 spec→roadmap \"orphan\" findings (4 files + 11 `Expect.*` predicate helpers) ARE present in `roadmap.md` — the files appear at `roadmap.md:102,142,409-412` and the 11 helpers are enumerated in row `COMP-010` at `roadmap.md:77`. The analyzer's bare-identifier string match missed qualified references (e.g., `Expect.jsonl.contains_event`) and tabular row contents.\n366\t- All 5 roadmap→spec \"orphan\" IDs (`D-1`, `D12`, `D3`, `D5`, `D6`) are NOT present in `roadmap.md` as bare tokens at all. Only `D-5` and `D-8` (ADR labels) appear (`roadmap.md:57,86,109,344,348`). These are phantom matches from a buggy extraction regex, not real roadmap references.\n367\t\n368\tNet: 20/20 deviations are false-positives produced by the unimplemented classifier. Spec-fidelity is genuinely PASS.\n369\t\n370\t### Options\n371\t\n372\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n373\t|---|---|---|\n374\t| **A. Add `validation.unclassified_deviations: 20` and `validation.status: \"pass-with-triage\"`; document manual-triage policy here; produce `deviation-triage.md`; resolve `remediation-tasklist.md` per disposition.** | Preserves audit trail. Distinguishes \"fidelity failed\" from \"classifier unimplemented, manual triage performed.\" Unblocks Phase 1 execution. | Requires per-deviation manual evidence (done in `deviation-triage.md`). |\n375\t| B. Flip `validation.status` to `\"pass\"` and delete the deviation records. | Cleanest. | Destroys audit trail of what the analyzer flagged and why the maintainer decided each was a false-positive. |\n376\t| C. Leave the label as `\"fail\"` and block Phase 1 until the classifier is implemented (backlog item `pipeline-classifier-implementation`). | Lets the pipeline self-heal eventually. | Stalls the cliEval release on infrastructure the release itself doesn't need. The classifier is a separate roadmap concern. |\n377\t\n378\t### Decision: **A — pass-with-triage label, manual-triage policy, per-deviation disposition.**\n379\t\n380\t### Rationale\n381\t\n382\t1. The pipeline's 14 step statuses already provide an objective PASS signal; the post-pipeline validator's `\"fail\"` label is one composite indicator that conflates two distinct conditions (fidelity failure vs. unclassified records pending triage). Splitting them removes ambiguity for future readers.\n383\t2. Option B destroys the only artifact (`spec-deviations.md` + `deviation-registry.json`) that would let a future classifier replay the same input and verify it produces the same dispositions. Triage is durable; deletion is not.\n384\t3. Option C blocks an unrelated release on a tooling backlog item with no committed timeline. The cliEval release does not depend on classifier implementation — it only depends on the dispositions being recorded.\n385\t4. Manual-triage policy fits within ADR-lite scope: per-deviation rows with file:line evidence in `deviation-triage.md` are auditable, and `remediation-tasklist.md` updates close the loop with the original BLOCKING entries.\n386\t\n387\t### Manual-Triage Policy\n388\t\n389\tWhen `validation.status` is `\"fail\"` purely due to unclassified-classifier output AND `fidelity_status` is `\"pass\"` AND every pipeline `steps.*` is PASS, a maintainer MAY perform manual triage by:\n390\t\n391\t1. Reading each finding in `spec-deviations.md` plus its registry entry in `deviation-registry.json`.\n392\t2. Cross-referencing the cited symbol/file/ID against `roadmap.md` and `decisions.md` to confirm presence or absence.\n393\t3. Assigning one of the following dispositions:\n394\t   - `NO_ACTION` — finding is a false-positive (analyzer extraction artifact or naming-only mismatch); cite the roadmap.md line that contains the symbol.\n395\t   - `MAPPED → <ROADMAP_ROW_ID>` — finding maps to an existing roadmap row; cite the row.\n396\t   - `ADD_TO_P1` / `ADD_NEW_TASK` — genuine gap; do NOT silently edit phase tasklists; surface for separate gated decision.\n397\t   - `BLOCKING-CONFIRMED` — actual fidelity failure; halts the release.\n398\t4. Recording per-deviation dispositions in `deviation-triage.md` with file:line evidence.\n399\t5. Updating `remediation-tasklist.md` entries (`[x]` + disposition + evidence) and flipping `[UNCLASSIFIED]` markers in `spec-deviations.md` to their resolved classification.\n400\t\n401\t### Consequences\n402\t\n403\t- `.roadmap-state.json` SHOULD gain `validation.unclassified_deviations: 20` and `validation.status: \"pass-with-triage\"` once triage is complete. Per the user's instruction in this pre-execution-gate task, the JSON state file is NOT modified in this turn; the relabel is documented here as the authoritative semantic interpretation and is pending a follow-up edit.\n404\t- Future pipeline runs SHOULD distinguish \"fidelity FAIL\" from \"deviations pending triage\" via separate status fields rather than a single `validation.status` boolean.\n405\t- A backlog item should track implementing the deviation classifier so manual triage is no longer required (see `spec-deviations.md:10` reference to `pipeline-classifier-implementation`).\n406\t- The analyzer's roadmap→spec orphan ID extraction has a latent bug (phantom matches `D-1`, `D12`, `D3`, `D5`, `D6` that do not appear in roadmap.md); the classifier work should fix this regex.\n407\t- Phase-1 execution (`TASK-RF-20260518-cliEval-P1-pty-isolation-gates`) is UNBLOCKED upon triage completion.\n408\t\n409\t\n410\t---\n411\t\n412\t## D-10: NOTICE/LICENSE attribution mechanism for vendored ptytest (OQ-4 closure)\n413\t\n414\t**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n415\t**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n416\t**Roadmap cross-reference:** NFR-MAINT1 (roadmap row 23) — vendored ptytest fork setup; DOC-OQ4 (row 24) — NOTICE/LICENSE attribution; AC10 (row 25) — fork SHA pin + drift policy.\n417\t\n418\t### Context\n419\t\n420\t`OQ-4` (roadmap.md:173) asks: *\"Does the repo require a top-level NOTICE file for ptytest attribution?\"* Per debate convergence and roadmap row 132 (DOC-OQ4), OQ-4 is a hard M2 entry blocker — it must close before the vendored ptytest sources physically land under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` (T02.01 / NFR-MAINT1). At the time this ADR lands, the repo has a top-level `LICENSE` (MIT) but no `NOTICE` file.\n421\t\n422\t`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` is MIT-licensed. The MIT License requires that \"the above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.\" We are vendoring a substantial portion of ptytest sources, so the upstream copyright + permission notice must travel with the distribution.\n423\t\n424\t`design-spec.md:785-787` already names the intended mechanism: retain the upstream `LICENSE` verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`, and reference it from a top-level `NOTICE`. This ADR ratifies that mechanism and records it as the OQ-4 closure artifact.\n425\t\n426\t### Options\n427\t\n428\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n429\t|---|---|---|\n430\t| **A. Top-level `NOTICE` referencing `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`; upstream LICENSE retained verbatim at the vendored path; PROVENANCE.md records fork SHA + changes.** | Industry-standard pattern (matches Apache 2 / Google convention even though both licenses here are MIT). NOTICE is grep-able by license-scan tooling. Survives upstream skill/template churn because the NOTICE points at a path the project controls. | Adds one new top-level file. |\n431\t| B. Inline ptytest MIT terms into the repo root `LICENSE` as an appendix. | One fewer file. | Mixes IronClaude's own copyright with a third-party copyright in the same file — confusing for downstream license scanners, and the precedent generalizes badly when more third-party components arrive. |\n432\t| C. Rely solely on `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` without a top-level NOTICE. | Minimum disruption to existing tree. | Fails the convention that license tooling (e.g., FOSSA, REUSE, OSS Review Toolkit) scans the repo root for NOTICE-style attribution before recursing. Maintainer audit trail is weaker. |\n433\t\n434\t### Decision: **A — top-level `NOTICE` references `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`; upstream LICENSE retained verbatim at the vendored path.**\n435\t\n436\t### Rationale\n437\t\n438\t1. The MIT obligation (\"retain the copyright notice and permission notice\") is satisfied by retaining the upstream LICENSE verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`. Adding a top-level `NOTICE` is **additive discipline** — it makes the attribution discoverable by repo-root scanners without dispersing the verbatim copyright across multiple files.\n439\t2. The `NOTICE` mechanism generalizes: when future third-party components are vendored (or when the harness adds an additional dependency that requires attribution), they get a new section in `NOTICE` and a verbatim LICENSE at their vendored path. Option B (inline appendix) does not generalize as cleanly.\n440\t3. Option C (no NOTICE) would force every reader and every license-scan tool to traverse the source tree to discover third-party content. The marginal cost of one top-level file is small; the marginal benefit (audit ergonomics) is real.\n441\t4. The `NOTICE` file does not change the license terms of either IronClaude or ptytest. Both remain MIT. `NOTICE` is documentation of attribution, not a license modification.\n442\t\n443\t### Closure of OQ-4\n444\t\n445\t- **Question:** Does the repo require a top-level NOTICE file for ptytest attribution?\n446\t- **Resolution:** YES. A top-level `NOTICE` MUST exist before T02.01 lands vendored ptytest sources. The `NOTICE` references `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` (the verbatim upstream MIT terms) and `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` (fork SHA + changes).\n447\t- **Resolution status:** RESOLVED — 2026-05-20.\n448\t- **Resolution artifact:** `NOTICE` at repo root (this commit). The referenced `LICENSE` and `PROVENANCE.md` under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` land with T02.01 (NFR-MAINT1).\n449\t\n450\t### Attribution clause (canonical text)\n451\t\n452\tThe `NOTICE` file contains the following authoritative attribution clause for ptytest. A fork of `brandon-fryslie/ptytest` is vendored under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` and used as the PTY/pexpect driver layer for the CLI evaluation harness. The eval harness needs a PTY (pseudo-terminal) driver that can spawn it, write to its stdin, read from its stdout/stderr, detect \"prompt ready\" states, kill on timeout, and capture exit codes.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons | LOC | Risk |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **A. Fork `brandon-fryslie/ptytest`** | Working Claude Code example already exists in repo. Plugin-free standalone class. Active enough (recent commits). | 1 star / 0 forks → bus-factor real. Includes pytest-fixture machinery we don't need. | ~300 LOC adaptation | LOW (ptytest is small, easy to own) |\n| B. Build minimal `pexpect` wrapper from scratch | Clean ownership, no upstream-drift risk, exactly the API we want. | More code to write, debug, test. We re-derive Claude Code TTY quirks. | ~700 LOC fresh | MEDIUM (pexpect has subtle gotchas) |\n| C. Use `pexpect` directly (no wrapper) | Zero abstraction overhead. | Every eval re-implements spawn/wait/expect_prompt patterns; code duplication. | ~0 LOC harness; +~50 LOC per eval | HIGH (drift across evals) |\n\n### Decision: **A — fork ptytest, vendor under `cli/eval/pty/`, fork-and-own posture.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The fork-research report verified ptytest has a working Claude Code example, which means the hardest TTY edge cases (prompt detection, ANSI escape handling, line-buffered reads) are already solved.\n2. Bus-factor risk is mitigated by **vendoring** (we copy the code in; no runtime pip dependency on the upstream repo). If upstream dies, we have a frozen working copy.\n3. The durable dependency is `pexpect` (2.8k stars, active again at v4.9) — ptytest is just a thin layer on top.\n4. 300 LOC adaptation is ~5x cheaper than 700 LOC fresh build.\n5. Option C (raw pexpect) fails the harness-cleanliness goal — evals should look like assertion code, not PTY plumbing.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- We own a small vendored fork forever. `PROVENANCE.md` documents the fork SHA and our diffs.\n- Quarterly review of upstream `pexpect` releases; resync if security-relevant.\n- A new file at `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` reproducing upstream MIT terms (license compliance).\n\n---\n\n## D-2: Assertion DSL — adopt `Expect.*` port vs raw assertions\n\n**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n**Roadmap cross-reference:** COMP-010 (roadmap row 12) — ExpectDSL interface in M1; FR-EXP1 (row 64) — Expect.* primitives in M4; COMP-010.1..COMP-010.6 (rows 65–70) — per-primitive contracts.\n\n### Context\n\nEach eval needs to verify hook side-effects (file presence, JSONL events, settings.json registrations, exit codes, TTY output). The expression of these assertions can be:\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Port `mcp-eval`'s `Expect.tools.*` DSL idea as Python primitives** | Declarative YAML manifest possible. Reusable across evals. Single place to add new assertion types. Mental-model match with industry pattern. | +~200 LOC for the DSL itself. Some abstraction overhead (declarative → callable). |\n| B. Raw Python `assert` statements in each eval | Lowest abstraction. No new code. | Each eval is more code. Declarative YAML manifest not possible (or only partial). Failure messages are ad-hoc per eval. |\n| C. Use pytest's assertion machinery directly | Free; idiomatic. | Couples harness to pytest, but the harness is NOT pytest-driven (it's a CLI subcommand). |\n\n### Decision: **A — port `Expect.*` DSL, ~200 LOC, no upstream dependency.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The YAML manifest schema (§5 of design-spec) is a hard requirement to keep eval definitions declarative and reviewable. Raw Python assertions can't be expressed in YAML.\n2. A small DSL (~10 method types: `file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) covers all 15 evals comfortably.\n3. The mcp-eval API surface is a proven mental model — we steal the ergonomics without taking a dependency on the framework (which has the wrong architecture for our needs).\n4. Failure messages from a typed DSL are uniformly high-quality (the DSL knows its own structure); ad-hoc assertions tend to produce noisy error output.\n5. The DSL has a programmatic escape hatch for parameterized evals that can't be expressed in YAML — so we don't lock ourselves out of complex assertions.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `cli/eval/expect.py` is a public API surface; changes need backward-compat care once evals are written against it.\n- The YAML schema (`suites/suite.schema.json`) and the DSL must stay in lockstep — `loader.py` translates declarative → callable.\n- Out-of-scope for v1: LLM-judge assertions (mcp-eval has these; we don't need them for hook validation).\n\n---\n\n## D-3: HOME isolation — extend existing `IsolationLayers` vs separate class\n\n**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n**Roadmap cross-reference:** FR-ISO1 (roadmap row 28) — HomeIsolation extends IsolationLayers; COMP-006 (row 32) — HomeIsolation implementation; FR-ISO2 (row 29) — path containment guard composed alongside.\n\n### Context\n\n`src/superclaude/cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182` already implements 4-layer per-subprocess isolation (`CLAUDE_WORK_DIR`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DIR`, `CLAUDE_SETTINGS_DIR`). The eval harness needs a 5th layer: `HOME` override (and `XDG_*` companions). Question: extend the existing class or build a new one?\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Compose `IsolationLayers` in a new `HomeIsolation` class** | Reuses 4 existing layers for free. No risk to sprint's existing usage. New `HOME` layer lives in eval code, not sprint code. | Two classes to maintain. Slight indirection. |\n| B. Add `HOME` layer to `IsolationLayers` directly (modify sprint code) | One unified class. Sprint also benefits from HOME isolation (theoretical future use). | Modifies code outside this release's scope. Sprint doesn't currently need HOME isolation, so YAGNI risk. Sprint regression risk. |\n| C. Build `HomeIsolation` standalone (no inheritance/composition) | Maximum decoupling. | Duplicate 4 layers of code. Bug-fixes diverge over time. |\n\n### Decision: **A — `HomeIsolation` COMPOSES `IsolationLayers` (has-a, not is-a).**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. Composition preserves sprint's existing class verbatim. Zero risk to PR #49-era infrastructure.\n2. The `HOME` layer is conceptually a \"5th layer,\" but its lifecycle (mkdtemp → install hooks → seed state → teardown) is more complex than the existing 4 (which are just env-var settings). Keeping it separate cleanly captures that asymmetry.\n3. If a future sprint feature ever needs HOME isolation, it can either re-use `HomeIsolation` directly or migrate the logic upward — easier path than splitting later.\n4. The interface in `design-spec.md` §7 already reflects this: `HomeIsolation` exposes `.env()` that returns the dict overlay, which the caller merges with `IsolationLayers.env()` output.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `HomeIsolation` constructor takes an `IsolationLayers` instance as a dependency.\n- Documentation in `cli/eval/isolation.py` cross-references `cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182`.\n- If the sprint `IsolationLayers` API ever changes shape, we have a single integration point to update.\n\n---\n\n## D-4: Eval registry — YAML manifest vs Python-decorator-registered\n\n**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n**Roadmap cross-reference:** DM-011 (roadmap row 2) — Suite manifest YAML schema; DM-002 (row 3) — EvalSpec model; FR-SCH1 (row 4) — manifest schema validation; COMP-002 (row 6) — SuiteLoader.\n\n### Context\n\nThe 15 evals need to be defined somewhere. The orchestrator needs to enumerate them, validate dependencies, schedule them, and report results. Options diverge on where the \"source of truth\" lives.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. YAML manifests under `suites/*.yaml`** | Declarative, human-readable, reviewable diffs, schema-validated, easy to add new suites (`fast.yaml`, `nightly.yaml`). Non-Python contributors can author/review evals. | Some Python required for complex pre/post logic; needs an \"escape hatch\" mechanism. |\n| B. Python-decorator-registered (`@eval(id=\"E1\", title=\"...\", requires=[...])`) | Maximum flexibility — eval bodies are first-class Python. Type-checking friendly. | Diff churn higher (Python > YAML in PR review noise). Adding new suites requires more boilerplate. Schema enforcement is via type hints, less ergonomic for non-Python contributors. |\n| C. Hybrid — YAML defines metadata, Python `@assertion_callback` for escape hatch | Best of both worlds for simple+complex evals. | More mechanism to learn; two source-of-truth surfaces. |\n\n### Decision: **A — YAML manifest as primary, with optional Python escape-hatch via `callback:` field for parameterized/complex evals.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. **Reviewability** is paramount: a PR adding a new eval should be readable as a YAML diff, not as a Python class with overrides. The 15-eval suite is meant to be a living spec — declarative beats imperative.\n2. The schema (§5 of design-spec) covers the common case (file/JSONL/settings/exit/stderr/duration assertions) elegantly.\n3. The `callback:` field provides escape-hatch power without forcing every eval through it:\n   ```yaml\n   - id: E14\n     title: \"concurrent SessionStart bursts\"\n     callback: superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start\n   ```\n   The callback is invoked only when an eval's logic can't be expressed in YAML (E14, possibly E15).\n4. New suites (`fast.yaml`, `regression.yaml`, etc.) drop in as data files with no code changes — exactly the kind of low-friction extension we want.\n5. `loader.py` validates against `suites/suite.schema.json` once; the rest of the pipeline trusts validated input.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `suites/suite.schema.json` is a public artifact (committed) — schema changes need versioning.\n- Callbacks live in `suites/<suite-name>_callbacks.py` co-located with the YAML.\n- Eval IDs (`E1`, `E2.1`, etc.) are stable identifiers — must not be renamed without a migration note.\n- Parameterized evals (e.g., E2's 3 prefix variants) use a `parameterize:` block in YAML; the loader expands these into separate `EvalSpec` instances at load time.\n\n---\n\n## D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate — falsifiable definition of \"equivalent regression\"\n\n**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n**Roadmap cross-reference:** FR-G5 (roadmap row 75) — falsifiable hook-matcher coverage gate CLI entry; TEST-013 (row 101) — coverage-gate tests; MIG-002 (row 103) — eval-batch rollout references coverage map.\n\n### Context\n\nGoal G5 in design-spec §1 said \"catch the bug PR #49 fixed and any equivalent future regression\" — but \"equivalent\" was undefined. Spec-panel finding W-1 (CRITICAL) flagged this as unfalsifiable: with no measurable acceptance criterion, the harness has no way to answer \"did I do my job?\"\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Coverage gate: every matcher P in `hooks.json` must have ≥1 eval in the loaded suite** | Falsifiable, mechanically enforceable. CI-friendly. Maps to the actual class of bugs PR #49 lived in (matcher↔case-body drift). | Requires `eval doctor --check-coverage`. New eval must accompany every new hook matcher. |\n| B. Snapshot match — assert this commit's matchers equal a frozen golden list | Simple. | Frozen list goes stale; bug-class definition is \"matches a list\" not \"real-world hook fires.\" |\n| C. Leave G5 prose-only (\"intent is clear enough\") | Zero work. | Spec-panel CRITICAL stands; downstream tasklist has no test for G5. |\n\n### Decision: **A — coverage gate enforced by loader + `eval doctor --check-coverage`.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The class of bug PR #49 introduced was \"matcher exists in `hooks.json` but no real-world test exercises it.\" A coverage gate directly inverts that condition.\n2. Mechanical enforcement at load time means the harness fails fast (exit 2) when a new matcher lands without an eval — no silent drift.\n3. The gate runs in two places: (a) `eval doctor` for pre-flight, (b) top of `eval run` for the live run. Both are cheap (single pass over `hooks.json` + manifest).\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `loader.py` reads `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` at run-start, extracts every PostToolUse matcher pattern, and verifies ≥1 eval in the manifest declares `inputs.expect_tool_call` matching each pattern.\n- New hook matcher PRs must include a paired eval — enforced by a CI lint (deferred per non-goals but the harness itself enforces locally).\n- Acceptable escape hatch: manifest declares `coverage_exempt_matchers: [\"<pattern>\"]` with a `reason:` field for matchers intentionally untestable.\n\n---\n\n## D-6: Disk-budget enforcement strategy\n\n**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n**Roadmap cross-reference:** NFR-PERF4 (roadmap row 60) — `--max-disk-mb` disk-budget enforcement at 5s polling; COMP-003 (row 57) — RunOrchestrator hosts DiskBudgetWatchdog sidecar.\n\n### Context\n\nSpec-panel finding N-2 (CRITICAL) flagged internal inconsistency: §14 R4 cited `--max-disk-mb` as the mitigation but §4 flag table omitted the flag. Either add it or strike the mitigation.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Add `--max-disk-mb` flag (default 1024), poll every 5s, abort-on-breach** | Closes the cited risk. Bounded blast radius. Simple implementation. | One more flag. Polling adds tiny CPU overhead. |\n| B. Strike the mitigation from R4; document \"user must monitor disk manually\" | Zero code. | R4 becomes a known accepted risk; eval runs on a constrained box can fill disk. |\n| C. Hard limit via `setrlimit(RLIMIT_FSIZE)` on each subprocess | Kernel enforced. | Per-process limit, not cumulative; doesn't catch the 15-parallel-evals case. |\n\n### Decision: **A — add `--max-disk-mb` flag with 5-second polling.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The risk is real: 15 parallel evals × keep-home-on-failure × TTY transcripts can fill a small disk silently. A polling guard with graceful abort (in-flight evals complete, new ones blocked) preserves results-so-far.\n2. 5-second polling is cheap: one `du -sb` (or Python `os.walk` accumulator) per tick. At 15-parallel, the orchestrator is mostly blocked on pexpect anyway.\n3. Default of 1024 MB matches design-spec §12 sizing (~300 MB total artifacts at 15-eval max-keep). Generous but not unbounded.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- New flag `--max-disk-mb INT` in §4 flag table; default 1024; `0` disables.\n- New orchestrator component `DiskBudgetWatchdog` running in a sidecar thread.\n- New exit-2 reason code: `disk_budget_exceeded`.\n- Summary.json gains `harness.peak_disk_mb` field (also satisfies spec-panel H-1 partial).\n\n---\n\n## D-7: Path-traversal hardening for HomeIsolation\n\n**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n**Roadmap cross-reference:** FR-SCH2 (roadmap row 5) — eval_id regex guard; FR-ISO2 (row 29) — path containment guard; AC12 (row 16) — allowed scratch roots; NFR-SEC1 (row 7) — path-traversal negative-case tests.\n\n### Context\n\nSpec-panel finding Wh-1 (CRITICAL) demonstrated that a malformed `eval_id` (e.g., `../../../etc/passwd` or `{session_id}` self-reference) could cause `HomeIsolation` to write outside the scratch root. The §14 R7 \"Hard guard\" was prose-only and ambiguous about whether `..` traversal was resolved before the containment check.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Defense-in-depth: loader regex + HomeIsolation `Path.resolve().is_relative_to()` + symlink-resolution after creation + scratch_root allowlist** | Catches the attack at three independent layers. Fails closed at every layer. | More code (~30 LOC). Three checks to maintain. |\n| B. Loader-only regex validation | Simple. | Single layer = single failure point. |\n| C. Trust the manifest author (no validation) | Zero work. | Manifest is data; data ingestion without validation is the original sin of basically every security incident. |\n\n### Decision: **A — three-layer defense-in-depth (regex + Path containment + symlink resolution + allowlist).**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The harness operates on user-supplied YAML and writes to disk. This is the textbook injection surface. Single-layer validation is industry-known-insufficient.\n2. Each layer catches a distinct attack class: regex blocks `..` and template tokens; `Path.resolve()` normalizes any survivor; `is_relative_to(scratch_root)` enforces containment; symlink resolution catches the \"scratch dir is a symlink to `$HOME`\" race; allowlist on `scratch_root` itself blocks `--output-dir /home/user/.claude`.\n3. Python 3.10+ (project minimum) provides `Path.is_relative_to()` natively.\n4. The performance cost is negligible (4 stat calls per eval setup).\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `HomeIsolation.setup()` gains a documented pre-flight sequence (regex re-check → resolve → contain → symlink-resolve → write).\n- `EvalConfig` gains an `allowed_scratch_roots: tuple[Path, ...]` field, defaulting to `(/tmp/eval-runs, <repo>/.dev/eval-runs)`.\n- New exception type `HomeContainmentViolation` (subclass of `EvalHarnessError`); maps to status ERRORED.\n- Test `test_isolation.py` MUST include: (a) `..` traversal, (b) absolute-path eval_id, (c) symlink-to-home attack, (d) `--output-dir` outside allowlist.\n\n---\n\n## D-8: Reporter dimensional invariant (consumes N', not K)\n\n**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n**Roadmap cross-reference:** COMP-008 (roadmap row 55) — Reporter / AggregatedRunReport; FR-RPT1 (row 54) — aggregated run report dimensional invariant; COMP-003 (row 57) — RunOrchestrator emits one EvalOutcome per expanded EvalSpec.\n\n### Context\n\nSpec-panel Pipeline-CRITICAL showed §9's summary.json example reported `14 passed, 1 failed, 0 skipped` (totals = 15, the full suite) but the spec text described capability filtering as removing evals from the schedule. If the reporter only sees the K-kept subset, a `--no-mcp` run that drops 5 evals would show \"10 passed\" with no audit trail of the 5 skipped — a silent regression in coverage measurement.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Orchestrator emits N' EvalOutcomes (one per expanded EvalSpec); SKIPPED carries reason; reporter asserts `len(outcomes) == N'`** | Audit trail preserved. Coverage queries answerable. Contract is checkable. | Slightly more state per skipped eval. |\n| B. Reporter takes K outcomes + a separate skipped-list; merges at render time | Same audit trail. | Two channels to keep in sync. More places to drop a skip. |\n| C. Status quo (K only, skip count is metadata) | Less code. | The spec-panel CRITICAL stands; this is the bug being filed. |\n\n### Decision: **A — single list of N' EvalOutcome records; reporter asserts dimensional invariant.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. A single dataclass list eliminates the \"two channels in sync\" failure mode of option B.\n2. The contract assertion (`len(outcomes) == counts.expanded_n_prime`) makes the invariant grep-able and test-able; CI can verify it doesn't regress.\n3. Status taxonomy expansion (8 statuses with explicit FAIL/ERRORED/TIMEOUT/INTERRUPTED/SKIPPED/XFAIL/XPASS distinction) is a natural consequence — addresses the spec-panel \"failure-mode taxonomy is sparse\" consensus point.\n4. Performance cost is zero in practice (the SKIPPED records are empty-shell; N' is at most 17 with parameterize expansion).\n\n### Consequences\n\n- New dataclass `EvalOutcome` in `models.py` (see design-spec §9 \"Aggregator data contract\").\n- New status values `ERRORED`, `INTERRUPTED`, `XFAIL`, `XPASS` (in addition to PASS/FAIL/TIMEOUT/SKIPPED) — taxonomy frozen in design-spec §9.\n- New error `ReporterContractViolation` raised on dimensional mismatch at `AggregatedRunReport.from_outcomes()`.\n- Summary.json gains `counts` block (manifest_n, expanded_n_prime, kept_k, skipped_s).\n- Exit-code mapping in §4 cross-references this taxonomy.\n\n---\n\n## Cross-cutting decision: implementation order\n\n(Not a strict ADR, but worth recording.) Per §17 of `design-spec.md`, the implementation phases are:\n\n1. **Phase 1** (1 day): vendored `pty/`, `HomeIsolation`, capability gates, `eval doctor`. Smoke: `superclaude eval doctor` green.\n2. **Phase 2** (1 day): `loader.py`, `models.py`, `expect.py`, `eval describe/list`. Smoke: parse a 1-eval manifest.\n3. **Phase 3** (1 day): `orchestrator.py`, `runner.py`, `reporter.py`, `eval run`. Smoke: 1 real Claude Code session driven end-to-end.\n4. **Phase 4** (0.5 day): wire into `cli/main.py`, Makefile target, .gitignore. Smoke: `make verify-sync` still EXIT=0.\n5. **Phase 5** (1-2 weeks): implement E1-E15 in 3-5 PR batches.\n\n**Rationale for this ordering:** Phase 1 is the riskiest (PTY mechanics). Validating the harness can spawn-and-observe ONE Claude Code session is the gate that determines whether the entire approach is viable. If Phase 1 fails (e.g., Claude Code's TTY behavior breaks pexpect-style detection), we re-pivot before committing to the rest of the architecture.\n\n---\n\n## Sign-off\n\n| Decision | Status | Signed | Date |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| D-1: PTY layer = fork ptytest | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-2: Assertion DSL = Expect.* port | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-3: HOME isolation = compose IsolationLayers | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-4: Eval registry = YAML + callback escape | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate (G5 falsifiable) | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-6: `--max-disk-mb` poller (R4 enforcement) | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-7: Three-layer path-traversal hardening | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-8: Reporter consumes N' + status taxonomy | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-10: NOTICE/LICENSE attribution for vendored ptytest | 🟢 APPROVED (R5) | RyanW | 2026-05-20 |\n\n**Maintainer:** sign-off pass complete (R5, 2026-05-20). All 8 ADRs plus D-10 approved; SC1 acceptance criterion satisfied. Future revisions follow the \"Reject/revise\" rule below.\n\n**Reject/revise:** add a row to a \"Revisions\" section below; do not edit the original decisions in-place.\n\n---\n\n## Revisions\n\n(none yet)\n\n---\n\n## D-9: Reconcile `validation.status: \"fail\"` label and document manual-triage policy for unclassified deviations\n\n### Context\n\n`.roadmap-state.json:113-117` reports `fidelity_status: \"pass\"` and `validation.status: \"fail\"` (timestamp 2026-05-18T19:47:24Z, six minutes after the final pipeline step completed). However, every pipeline step in `steps.*` — including `spec-fidelity`, `wiring-verification`, `deviation-analysis`, and `remediate` — recorded `status: \"PASS\"`. `spec-fidelity.md:1-7` likewise records 0 HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW deviations and PASS convergence.\n\nThe `validation.status: \"fail\"` label was stamped by a separate validator step that reacts to the 20 records in `spec-deviations.md`. Per `spec-deviations.md:10`, \"deviation classification is not yet implemented. All records currently render as UNCLASSIFIED.\" `remediation-tasklist.md:14-34` then escalates all 20 to `BLOCKING` with owner `UNKNOWN` purely because the classifier never ran — not because spec-fidelity actually regressed.\n\nA manual cross-check (performed during this reconciliation) confirms:\n\n- All 15 spec→roadmap \"orphan\" findings (4 files + 11 `Expect.*` predicate helpers) ARE present in `roadmap.md` — the files appear at `roadmap.md:102,142,409-412` and the 11 helpers are enumerated in row `COMP-010` at `roadmap.md:77`. The analyzer's bare-identifier string match missed qualified references (e.g., `Expect.jsonl.contains_event`) and tabular row contents.\n- All 5 roadmap→spec \"orphan\" IDs (`D-1`, `D12`, `D3`, `D5`, `D6`) are NOT present in `roadmap.md` as bare tokens at all. Only `D-5` and `D-8` (ADR labels) appear (`roadmap.md:57,86,109,344,348`). These are phantom matches from a buggy extraction regex, not real roadmap references.\n\nNet: 20/20 deviations are false-positives produced by the unimplemented classifier. Spec-fidelity is genuinely PASS.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Add `validation.unclassified_deviations: 20` and `validation.status: \"pass-with-triage\"`; document manual-triage policy here; produce `deviation-triage.md`; resolve `remediation-tasklist.md` per disposition.** | Preserves audit trail. Distinguishes \"fidelity failed\" from \"classifier unimplemented, manual triage performed.\" Unblocks Phase 1 execution. | Requires per-deviation manual evidence (done in `deviation-triage.md`). |\n| B. Flip `validation.status` to `\"pass\"` and delete the deviation records. | Cleanest. | Destroys audit trail of what the analyzer flagged and why the maintainer decided each was a false-positive. |\n| C. Leave the label as `\"fail\"` and block Phase 1 until the classifier is implemented (backlog item `pipeline-classifier-implementation`). | Lets the pipeline self-heal eventually. | Stalls the cliEval release on infrastructure the release itself doesn't need. The classifier is a separate roadmap concern. |\n\n### Decision: **A — pass-with-triage label, manual-triage policy, per-deviation disposition.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The pipeline's 14 step statuses already provide an objective PASS signal; the post-pipeline validator's `\"fail\"` label is one composite indicator that conflates two distinct conditions (fidelity failure vs. unclassified records pending triage). Splitting them removes ambiguity for future readers.\n2. Option B destroys the only artifact (`spec-deviations.md` + `deviation-registry.json`) that would let a future classifier replay the same input and verify it produces the same dispositions. Triage is durable; deletion is not.\n3. Option C blocks an unrelated release on a tooling backlog item with no committed timeline. The cliEval release does not depend on classifier implementation — it only depends on the dispositions being recorded.\n4. Manual-triage policy fits within ADR-lite scope: per-deviation rows with file:line evidence in `deviation-triage.md` are auditable, and `remediation-tasklist.md` updates close the loop with the original BLOCKING entries.\n\n### Manual-Triage Policy\n\nWhen `validation.status` is `\"fail\"` purely due to unclassified-classifier output AND `fidelity_status` is `\"pass\"` AND every pipeline `steps.*` is PASS, a maintainer MAY perform manual triage by:\n\n1. Reading each finding in `spec-deviations.md` plus its registry entry in `deviation-registry.json`.\n2. Cross-referencing the cited symbol/file/ID against `roadmap.md` and `decisions.md` to confirm presence or absence.\n3. Assigning one of the following dispositions:\n   - `NO_ACTION` — finding is a false-positive (analyzer extraction artifact or naming-only mismatch); cite the roadmap.md line that contains the symbol.\n   - `MAPPED → <ROADMAP_ROW_ID>` — finding maps to an existing roadmap row; cite the row.\n   - `ADD_TO_P1` / `ADD_NEW_TASK` — genuine gap; do NOT silently edit phase tasklists; surface for separate gated decision.\n   - `BLOCKING-CONFIRMED` — actual fidelity failure; halts the release.\n4. Recording per-deviation dispositions in `deviation-triage.md` with file:line evidence.\n5. Updating `remediation-tasklist.md` entries (`[x]` + disposition + evidence) and flipping `[UNCLASSIFIED]` markers in `spec-deviations.md` to their resolved classification.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `.roadmap-state.json` SHOULD gain `validation.unclassified_deviations: 20` and `validation.status: \"pass-with-triage\"` once triage is complete. Per the user's instruction in this pre-execution-gate task, the JSON state file is NOT modified in this turn; the relabel is documented here as the authoritative semantic interpretation and is pending a follow-up edit.\n- Future pipeline runs SHOULD distinguish \"fidelity FAIL\" from \"deviations pending triage\" via separate status fields rather than a single `validation.status` boolean.\n- A backlog item should track implementing the deviation classifier so manual triage is no longer required (see `spec-deviations.md:10` reference to `pipeline-classifier-implementation`).\n- The analyzer's roadmap→spec orphan ID extraction has a latent bug (phantom matches `D-1`, `D12`, `D3`, `D5`, `D6` that do not appear in roadmap.md); the classifier work should fix this regex.\n- Phase-1 execution (`TASK-RF-20260518-cliEval-P1-pty-isolation-gates`) is UNBLOCKED upon triage completion.\n\n\n---\n\n## D-10: NOTICE/LICENSE attribution mechanism for vendored ptytest (OQ-4 closure)\n\n**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n**Roadmap cross-reference:** NFR-MAINT1 (roadmap row 23) — vendored ptytest fork setup; DOC-OQ4 (row 24) — NOTICE/LICENSE attribution; AC10 (row 25) — fork SHA pin + drift policy.\n\n### Context\n\n`OQ-4` (roadmap.md:173) asks: *\"Does the repo require a top-level NOTICE file for ptytest attribution?\"* Per debate convergence and roadmap row 132 (DOC-OQ4), OQ-4 is a hard M2 entry blocker — it must close before the vendored ptytest sources physically land under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` (T02.01 / NFR-MAINT1). At the time this ADR lands, the repo has a top-level `LICENSE` (MIT) but no `NOTICE` file.\n\n`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` is MIT-licensed. The MIT License requires that \"the above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.\" We are vendoring a substantial portion of ptytest sources, so the upstream copyright + permission notice must travel with the distribution.\n\n`design-spec.md:785-787` already names the intended mechanism: retain the upstream `LICENSE` verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`, and reference it from a top-level `NOTICE`. This ADR ratifies that mechanism and records it as the OQ-4 closure artifact.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Top-level `NOTICE` referencing `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`; upstream LICENSE retained verbatim at the vendored path; PROVENANCE.md records fork SHA + changes.** | Industry-standard pattern (matches Apache 2 / Google convention even though both licenses here are MIT). NOTICE is grep-able by license-scan tooling. Survives upstream skill/template churn because the NOTICE points at a path the project controls. | Adds one new top-level file. |\n| B. Inline ptytest MIT terms into the repo root `LICENSE` as an appendix. | One fewer file. | Mixes IronClaude's own copyright with a third-party copyright in the same file — confusing for downstream license scanners, and the precedent generalizes badly when more third-party components arrive. |\n| C. Rely solely on `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` without a top-level NOTICE. | Minimum disruption to existing tree. | Fails the convention that license tooling (e.g., FOSSA, REUSE, OSS Review Toolkit) scans the repo root for NOTICE-style attribution before recursing. Maintainer audit trail is weaker. |\n\n### Decision: **A — top-level `NOTICE` references `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`; upstream LICENSE retained verbatim at the vendored path.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The MIT obligation (\"retain the copyright notice and permission notice\") is satisfied by retaining the upstream LICENSE verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`. Adding a top-level `NOTICE` is **additive discipline** — it makes the attribution discoverable by repo-root scanners without dispersing the verbatim copyright across multiple files.\n2. The `NOTICE` mechanism generalizes: when future third-party components are vendored (or when the harness adds an additional dependency that requires attribution), they get a new section in `NOTICE` and a verbatim LICENSE at their vendored path. Option B (inline appendix) does not generalize as cleanly.\n3. Option C (no NOTICE) would force every reader and every license-scan tool to traverse the source tree to discover third-party content. The marginal cost of one top-level file is small; the marginal benefit (audit ergonomics) is real.\n4. The `NOTICE` file does not change the license terms of either IronClaude or ptytest. Both remain MIT. `NOTICE` is documentation of attribution, not a license modification.\n\n### Closure of OQ-4\n\n- **Question:** Does the repo require a top-level NOTICE file for ptytest attribution?\n- **Resolution:** YES. A top-level `NOTICE` MUST exist before T02.01 lands vendored ptytest sources. The `NOTICE` references `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` (the verbatim upstream MIT terms) and `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` (fork SHA + changes).\n- **Resolution status:** RESOLVED — 2026-05-20.\n- **Resolution artifact:** `NOTICE` at repo root (this commit). The referenced `LICENSE` and `PROVENANCE.md` under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` land with T02.01 (NFR-MAINT1).\n\n### Attribution clause (canonical text)\n\nThe `NOTICE` file contains the following authoritative attribution clause for ptytest. Any future edit to this clause SHOULD update both the `NOTICE` file and this ADR section in lockstep:\n\n> **ptytest (vendored fork)** — Upstream: `https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest`; License: MIT; Location: `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`. A fork of `brandon-fryslie/ptytest` is vendored under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` and used as the PTY/pexpect driver layer for the CLI evaluation harness. The upstream MIT LICENSE is retained verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`, and `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` records the fork SHA, vendoring date, and the changes made relative to upstream.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- A new file `NOTICE` exists at the repository root. Purpose\n\nOQ-2 (roadmap.md:110) asks: *\"Concrete content of E3–E15 manifest entries.\"*\nThe design-spec (§5) shows only E1 and E2 parameterize as exemplars; E3–E15 are deferred.\n\nThis spec freezes the **inputs, expects, and capability tags** for each of the 13 remaining bodies so that T05.07 … T05.21 may author them without further design churn.\n\n## 2. Design constraints\n\n1. **Coverage:** every hook event type in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` MUST be exercised by ≥1 eval among {E1, E2.1–3, E3 … E15}.\n2. **Determinism:** every body MUST pass deterministically on a clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) — no time-of-day, no network, no shared mutable state. (3-run determinism is the per-task AC.)\n3. **No OQ-8 dependency:** E3–E15 MUST NOT require `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`. The original design-spec note tying E3 to \"30-min freshness tests\" is **superseded**; freshness-staleness via time offset is deferred until OQ-8 closes (separate follow-up).\n4. **Expect.* primitive only:** every body's assertions MUST be expressible via the v1 DSL (`file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) plus the YAML `callback:` escape (D-4) for E14.\n5. **Capability gating:** evals requiring a live MCP server soft-skip under `--no-mcp` with `skip_reason` populated (FR-CAP1).\n\n## 3. Hook surface coverage map\n\n| Hook event (hooks.json) | Matcher | Hook script | Covered by |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `SessionStart` (1st) | (none) | `session-init.sh` | **E3** |\n| `SessionStart` (2nd) | `*` | `freshness-session-start.sh` | **E4** |\n| `UserPromptSubmit` | (none) | `freshness-user-prompt.sh` | **E5** |\n| `PreToolUse` | `Edit\\|Write\\|mcp__serena__*` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | **E6** (Edit), **E7** (Write), **E8** (serena) |\n| `PostToolUse` | `Read` (async) | `freshness-post-read.sh` | **E9** |\n| `PostToolUse` | `mcp__auggie__\\|mcp__auggie-mcp__\\|mcp__airis-mcp-gateway__auggie_` | `auggie-flag-clear.sh` | **E1, E2.1, E2.2, E2.3** (existing) |\n| `SubagentStart` | (none) | `freshness-subagent-start.sh` | **E10** |\n| `SubagentStop` | (none) | `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` | **E11** |\n| Cross-cutting: deploy idempotency | — | `install_hooks` adapter | **E12** |\n| Cross-cutting: stderr fail-open | — | error path | **E13** |\n| Cross-cutting: concurrency | — | N parallel sessions | **E14** (YAML callback per D-4) |\n| Cross-cutting: timeout fail-open | — | `timeout:` field in hooks.json | **E15** (named in design-spec §11) |\n\nNet: 100% v1 hook-event coverage; 100% v1 PreToolUse matcher coverage; 100% v1 PostToolUse matcher coverage; cross-cutting fail-mode + concurrency tests included.\n\n## 4.
