The two evals are designed to\n32\tbe sibling pins on the sub-agent lifecycle and share the input shape\n33\t(spawn a sub-agent via the Task tool) while asserting different\n34\thook-event substrings.\n35\t\n36\tThe body must:\n37\t\n38\t- spawn a fresh claude session via the PTY harness, inject a prompt\n39\t  that invokes a sub-agent via the Task tool (`subagent_type='Explore'`\n40\t  per D-0082 §4 \"e.g., Explore or Plan\"), then exit cleanly so the\n41\t  PTY teardown reaps both the sub-agent and the async hook writer;\n42\t- assert the hook's observable side-effects (freshness ledger present\n43\t  + `subagent_start` event row);\n44\t- exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)` assertion can pin a clean\n45\t  `/quit` — and so the PTY teardown reaps the async writer (sibling\n46\t  pattern to E9);\n47\t- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the Task / Agent tool is\n48\t  a built-in Claude Code tool, not an MCP tool, so E10 runs on every\n49\t  host and under `--no-mcp`.\n50\t\n51\t## 2. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n52\t\n53\t`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStart block:\n54\t\n55\t```jsonc\n56\t\"SubagentStart\": [\n57\t  {\n58\t    \"hooks\": [\n59\t      { \"type\": \"command\",\n60\t        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-start.sh\",\n61\t        \"timeout\": 1,\n62\t        \"async\": true }\n63\t    ]\n64\t  }\n65\t]\n66\t```\n67\t\n68\tNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStart has no\n69\tmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent spawn fires the same single\n70\thook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched and E5\n71\tUserPromptSubmit-unmatched shapes (single-substring assertion, no\n72\tmatcher pin needed).\n73\t\n74\tThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E10 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\n75\tE10's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\n76\tbranch:\n77\t\n78\t| Observable | Purpose |\n79\t|---|---|\n80\t| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n81\t| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` | proves the `subagent_start`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n82\t| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n83\t\n84\tThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\n85\tthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\n86\tJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_start` substring pins that\n87\tthe SubagentStart hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\n88\tbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\n89\tshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n90\t\n91\tThe single-substring shape (`subagent_start`) differs from the\n92\ttwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\n93\tmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStart hook has **no\n94\tmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\n95\tto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` is therefore\n96\tsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\n97\tneeded (no sibling SubagentStart branches exist).\n98\t\n99\tThe D-0082 §4 row E10 specification phrases the secondary count\n100\tassertion as `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\n101\ttype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires:\n102\t\n103\t1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row;\n104\t2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"`;\n105\t3. Counting filtered rows and asserting `>= 1`.\n106\t\n107\tNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n108\t`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n109\t(`expect.py:269-369`). Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\n110\tdeferrals (event_count, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.15\n111\tlands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary substring assertion as a\n112\tproxy for the count assertion: if the substring is present at least\n113\tonce in `logs/freshness.jsonl`, then `event_count >= 1` is\n114\tnecessarily true. The strict `>= 1` (vs. `> 0`) form contributes no\n115\tadditional discrimination, so the substring proxy is exact for the\n116\toperationally-meaningful boundary.\n117\t\n118\t## 3. Frozen body shape\n119\t\n120\tThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E10 entry that\n121\tpreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\n122\ttitle `\"installer rejects unknown component class\"` from the\n123\tpre-OQ-2 numbering and no body). T05.15 replaces the scaffolding\n124\twith the frozen body. Final shape:\n125\t\n126\t| Field | Value |\n127\t|---|---|\n128\t| **title** | `\"SubagentStart hook fires\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E10) |\n129\t| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E9; was `installer` in stale stub) |\n130\t| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; Task / Agent tool is a built-in Claude Code tool, no MCP needed |\n131\t| **timeout_sec** | `60` (raised from defaults' 120s — two-prompt PTY round-trip is bounded by the Task tool round-trip + 1s async flush, typically <30s on a lightweight Explore-glob task; 60s is generous; matches E3/E4/E5/E6/E7/E8/E9 sibling for spawn-lifecycle parity) |\n132\t| **inputs[0].prompt** | Task fire: `\"Use the Task tool with subagent_type='Explore' to find files matching '*.md' under the current working directory and report the count.\"` + `expect_tool_call: Task` — the Task invocation that spawns the Explore sub-agent and fires the SubagentStart hook |\n133\t| **inputs[1].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract AND so the teardown reaps the async writer (and any in-flight sub-agent) |\n134\t| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n135\t| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"subagent_start\"' }` |\n136\t| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n137\t\n138\tPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\n139\tscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\n140\tper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n141\t\n142\tNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — Task / Agent is built into\n143\tClaude Code; no MCP server is required.\n144\t\n145\t**Footnote — event_count deferral.** D-0082 §4 row E10 lists a\n146\tsecondary assertion `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\n147\ttype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires a Python\n148\tcallable filter (`expect.py:269-369`) and is not expressible in\n149\tdeclarative YAML. Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\n150\tdeferrals, T05.15 lands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary\n151\tsubstring assertion as the operational proxy. The substring is\n152\tpresent iff at least one row carries `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`, so\n153\tthe `>= 1` boundary is exactly preserved by the proxy. The strict\n154\tdeclarative form remains deferred until either (a) the YAML callback\n155\tescape hatch (D-4) is exercised for E10, or (b) a future schema bump\n156\tadds a declarative `jsonl: { contains_event: { type: ... } }`\n157\tshorthand. Neither is in scope for T05.15.\n158\t\n159\tNote: invoking the Task tool fires the SubagentStart hook before the\n160\tsub-agent begins, and the SubagentStop hook after the sub-agent\n161\treturns. The `ts`,\n196\t  `session_id`, agent-type / agent-instance fields on the\n197\t  (post-update) JSONL row are not asserted against.\n198\t\n199\tThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E10` invocations on\n200\ta clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\n201\twhich is the per-task acceptance criterion.\n202\t\n203\t## 6. The\n210\t  `expect_tool_call` field on `inputs[0]` is accepted by the\n211\t  open-shape (mirrors E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 / E9 usage).\n212\t- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n213\t  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). The hook writer is a detached\n316\tbackground process whose flock-protected counter increment (and,\n317\tpost-script-update, freshness ledger append) runs out-of-band. The\n318\t`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\n319\tthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\n320\twriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n321\t+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n322\t\n323\tE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\n324\tthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\n325\tper §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\n326\toperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\n327\traces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\n328\tthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n329\t(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n330\t\n331\t### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n332\t\n333\tInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n334\t\n335\t1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n336\t   sub-agent process is spawned.\n337\t2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n338\t   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n339\t   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n340\t   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n341\t3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n342\t   to the parent session.\n343\t\n344\tE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. The SubagentStop\n345\tco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\n346\tthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`) appear somewhere\n347\tin the file — the subagent_stop row coexisting is benign.\n348\t\n349\tE11 (T05.16) will independently assert the paired\n350\t`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` substring + the count-equality\n351\t(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — the\n352\tlatter being a Python-callable predicate that follows the same\n353\tdeferral pattern established in §3 footnote.\n354\t\n355\t## 9. The two evals are designed to\nbe sibling pins on the sub-agent lifecycle and share the input shape\n(spawn a sub-agent via the Task tool) while asserting different\nhook-event substrings.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- spawn a fresh claude session via the PTY harness, inject a prompt\n  that invokes a sub-agent via the Task tool (`subagent_type='Explore'`\n  per D-0082 §4 \"e.g., Explore or Plan\"), then exit cleanly so the\n  PTY teardown reaps both the sub-agent and the async hook writer;\n- assert the hook's observable side-effects (freshness ledger present\n  + `subagent_start` event row);\n- exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)` assertion can pin a clean\n  `/quit` — and so the PTY teardown reaps the async writer (sibling\n  pattern to E9);\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the Task / Agent tool is\n  a built-in Claude Code tool, not an MCP tool, so E10 runs on every\n  host and under `--no-mcp`.\n\n## 2. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStart block:\n\n```jsonc\n\"SubagentStart\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-start.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 1,\n        \"async\": true }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStart has no\nmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent spawn fires the same single\nhook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched and E5\nUserPromptSubmit-unmatched shapes (single-substring assertion, no\nmatcher pin needed).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E10 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\nE10's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\nbranch:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` | proves the `subagent_start`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\nthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\nJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_start` substring pins that\nthe SubagentStart hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\nbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\nshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n\nThe single-substring shape (`subagent_start`) differs from the\ntwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\nmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStart hook has **no\nmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\nto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` is therefore\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\nneeded (no sibling SubagentStart branches exist).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 row E10 specification phrases the secondary count\nassertion as `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\ntype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires:\n\n1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row;\n2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"`;\n3. Counting filtered rows and asserting `>= 1`.\n\nNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\ndeferrals (event_count, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.15\nlands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary substring assertion as a\nproxy for the count assertion: if the substring is present at least\nonce in `logs/freshness.jsonl`, then `event_count >= 1` is\nnecessarily true. The strict `>= 1` (vs. `> 0`) form contributes no\nadditional discrimination, so the substring proxy is exact for the\noperationally-meaningful boundary.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E10 entry that\npreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\ntitle `\"installer rejects unknown component class\"` from the\npre-OQ-2 numbering and no body). T05.15 replaces the scaffolding\nwith the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"SubagentStart hook fires\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E10) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E9; was `installer` in stale stub) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; Task / Agent tool is a built-in Claude Code tool, no MCP needed |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (raised from defaults' 120s — two-prompt PTY round-trip is bounded by the Task tool round-trip + 1s async flush, typically <30s on a lightweight Explore-glob task; 60s is generous; matches E3/E4/E5/E6/E7/E8/E9 sibling for spawn-lifecycle parity) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | Task fire: `\"Use the Task tool with subagent_type='Explore' to find files matching '*.md' under the current working directory and report the count.\"` + `expect_tool_call: Task` — the Task invocation that spawns the Explore sub-agent and fires the SubagentStart hook |\n| **inputs[1].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract AND so the teardown reaps the async writer (and any in-flight sub-agent) |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"subagent_start\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — Task / Agent is built into\nClaude Code; no MCP server is required.\n\n**Footnote — event_count deferral.** D-0082 §4 row E10 lists a\nsecondary assertion `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\ntype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires a Python\ncallable filter (`expect.py:269-369`) and is not expressible in\ndeclarative YAML. Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\ndeferrals, T05.15 lands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary\nsubstring assertion as the operational proxy. The substring is\npresent iff at least one row carries `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`, so\nthe `>= 1` boundary is exactly preserved by the proxy. The strict\ndeclarative form remains deferred until either (a) the YAML callback\nescape hatch (D-4) is exercised for E10, or (b) a future schema bump\nadds a declarative `jsonl: { contains_event: { type: ... } }`\nshorthand. Neither is in scope for T05.15.\n\nNote: invoking the Task tool fires the SubagentStart hook before the\nsub-agent begins, and the SubagentStop hook after the sub-agent\nreturns. E10 only asserts the subagent_start substring; E11 (T05.16)\nwill independently assert the paired subagent_stop substring. Both\nsubstrings coexist in `logs/freshness.jsonl` (post-script-update);\nthe E10 assertion uses `Expect.file.contains` which only requires the\n`subagent_start` substring to appear somewhere, so the\nSubagentStop co-fire is harmless from E10's perspective.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E10` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E10` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17 evals\nunder suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n  SubagentStart fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n- The Task tool invocation triggers exactly one SubagentStart event\n  per the single Task prompt; the async hook emits one\n  `subagent_start`-typed counter increment (post-script-update, one\n  JSONL row with `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`).\n- The chosen sub-agent task (glob `*.md` under cwd) is read-only,\n  deterministic, and bounded — Explore will enumerate the per-eval\n  HOME's markdown files (typically zero on a clean ephemeral HOME)\n  and report the count, then return. No external network calls; no\n  shared-state dependencies.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after the async writer reaps.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n  `session_id`, agent-type / agent-instance fields on the\n  (post-update) JSONL row are not asserted against.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E10` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion.\n\n## 6. The two-element\n  `inputs[]` array is accepted by the open-shape array schema. The\n  `expect_tool_call` field on `inputs[0]` is accepted by the\n  open-shape (mirrors E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 / E9 usage).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). The hook writer is a detached\nbackground process whose flock-protected counter increment (and,\npost-script-update, freshness ledger append) runs out-of-band. The\n`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\nthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\nwriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n\nE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\nthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\nper §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\nraces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\nthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n\n### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n\nInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n\n1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n   sub-agent process is spawned.\n2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n   to the parent session.\n\nE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. The SubagentStop\nco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\nthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`) appear somewhere\nin the file — the subagent_stop row coexisting is benign.\n\nE11 (T05.16) will independently assert the paired\n`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` substring + the count-equality\n(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — the\nlatter being a Python-callable predicate that follows the same\ndeferral pattern established in §3 footnote.\n\n## 9. The\n318\t`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\n319\tthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\n320\twriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n321\t+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n322\t\n323\tE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\n324\tthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\n325\tper §3 footnote). On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n329\t(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n330\t\n331\t### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n332\t\n333\tInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n334\t\n335\t1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n336\t   sub-agent process is spawned.\n337\t2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n338\t   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n339\t   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n340\t   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n341\t3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n342\t   to the parent session.\n343\t\n344\tE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E10 — the eighth of the\npost-OQ-2 hook-event coverage entries (R-086 … R-098). E10 covers the\n**SubagentStart hook** in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` — whose\ncommand is `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (timeout=1, `async: true`,\nno matcher).\n\nE10 is the **second async hook eval** in the roster — after E9\n(D-0093 / PostToolUse Read async). Like E9, the matched hook is\ndeclared `async: true`, so the body relies on PTY teardown reaping\nthe async writer with the binary substring presence as the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee that the hook fired and flushed\nbefore session exit.\n\nE10 also opens the **sub-agent lifecycle pair**: E10 covers\nSubagentStart firing; E11 (D-0095 / T05.16) covers the matched\nSubagentStop firing with `event_count(subagent_start) ==\nevent_count(subagent_stop)` symmetry. The two evals are designed to\nbe sibling pins on the sub-agent lifecycle and share the input shape\n(spawn a sub-agent via the Task tool) while asserting different\nhook-event substrings.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- spawn a fresh claude session via the PTY harness, inject a prompt\n  that invokes a sub-agent via the Task tool (`subagent_type='Explore'`\n  per D-0082 §4 \"e.g., Explore or Plan\"), then exit cleanly so the\n  PTY teardown reaps both the sub-agent and the async hook writer;\n- assert the hook's observable side-effects (freshness ledger present\n  + `subagent_start` event row);\n- exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)` assertion can pin a clean\n  `/quit` — and so the PTY teardown reaps the async writer (sibling\n  pattern to E9);\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the Task / Agent tool is\n  a built-in Claude Code tool, not an MCP tool, so E10 runs on every\n  host and under `--no-mcp`.\n\n## 2. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStart block:\n\n```jsonc\n\"SubagentStart\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-start.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 1,\n        \"async\": true }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStart has no\nmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent spawn fires the same single\nhook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched and E5\nUserPromptSubmit-unmatched shapes (single-substring assertion, no\nmatcher pin needed).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E10 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\nE10's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\nbranch:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` | proves the `subagent_start`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\nthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\nJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_start` substring pins that\nthe SubagentStart hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\nbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\nshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n\nThe single-substring shape (`subagent_start`) differs from the\ntwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\nmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStart hook has **no\nmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\nto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` is therefore\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\nneeded (no sibling SubagentStart branches exist).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 row E10 specification phrases the secondary count\nassertion as `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\ntype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires:\n\n1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row;\n2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"`;\n3. Counting filtered rows and asserting `>= 1`.\n\nNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\ndeferrals (event_count, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.15\nlands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary substring assertion as a\nproxy for the count assertion: if the substring is present at least\nonce in `logs/freshness.jsonl`, then `event_count >= 1` is\nnecessarily true. The strict `>= 1` (vs. `> 0`) form contributes no\nadditional discrimination, so the substring proxy is exact for the\noperationally-meaningful boundary.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E10 entry that\npreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\ntitle `\"installer rejects unknown component class\"` from the\npre-OQ-2 numbering and no body). T05.15 replaces the scaffolding\nwith the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"SubagentStart hook fires\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E10) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E9; was `installer` in stale stub) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; Task / Agent tool is a built-in Claude Code tool, no MCP needed |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (raised from defaults' 120s — two-prompt PTY round-trip is bounded by the Task tool round-trip + 1s async flush, typically <30s on a lightweight Explore-glob task; 60s is generous; matches E3/E4/E5/E6/E7/E8/E9 sibling for spawn-lifecycle parity) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | Task fire: `\"Use the Task tool with subagent_type='Explore' to find files matching '*.md' under the current working directory and report the count.\"` + `expect_tool_call: Task` — the Task invocation that spawns the Explore sub-agent and fires the SubagentStart hook |\n| **inputs[1].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract AND so the teardown reaps the async writer (and any in-flight sub-agent) |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"subagent_start\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — Task / Agent is built into\nClaude Code; no MCP server is required.\n\n**Footnote — event_count deferral.** D-0082 §4 row E10 lists a\nsecondary assertion `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\ntype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires a Python\ncallable filter (`expect.py:269-369`) and is not expressible in\ndeclarative YAML. Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\ndeferrals, T05.15 lands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary\nsubstring assertion as the operational proxy. The substring is\npresent iff at least one row carries `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`, so\nthe `>= 1` boundary is exactly preserved by the proxy. The strict\ndeclarative form remains deferred until either (a) the YAML callback\nescape hatch (D-4) is exercised for E10, or (b) a future schema bump\nadds a declarative `jsonl: { contains_event: { type: ... } }`\nshorthand. Neither is in scope for T05.15.\n\nNote: invoking the Task tool fires the SubagentStart hook before the\nsub-agent begins, and the SubagentStop hook after the sub-agent\nreturns. E10 only asserts the subagent_start substring; E11 (T05.16)\nwill independently assert the paired subagent_stop substring. Both\nsubstrings coexist in `logs/freshness.jsonl` (post-script-update);\nthe E10 assertion uses `Expect.file.contains` which only requires the\n`subagent_start` substring to appear somewhere, so the\nSubagentStop co-fire is harmless from E10's perspective.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E10` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E10` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17 evals\nunder suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n  SubagentStart fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n- The Task tool invocation triggers exactly one SubagentStart event\n  per the single Task prompt; the async hook emits one\n  `subagent_start`-typed counter increment (post-script-update, one\n  JSONL row with `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`).\n- The chosen sub-agent task (glob `*.md` under cwd) is read-only,\n  deterministic, and bounded — Explore will enumerate the per-eval\n  HOME's markdown files (typically zero on a clean ephemeral HOME)\n  and report the count, then return. No external network calls; no\n  shared-state dependencies.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after the async writer reaps.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n  `session_id`, agent-type / agent-instance fields on the\n  (post-update) JSONL row are not asserted against.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E10` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion.\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`). The two-element\n  `inputs[]` array is accepted by the open-shape array schema. The\n  `expect_tool_call` field on `inputs[0]` is accepted by the\n  open-shape (mirrors E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 / E9 usage).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All three\n  primitives used (2×`file`, 1×`exit_code`) are in `PRIMITIVE_NAMES`\n  (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:186-265`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E10 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E10` (no flags) | RUNS | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E10 --no-mcp` | RUNS | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; Task tool is built-in |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E10 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E10 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9 (which also carry\n`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\ncapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. The hook writer is a detached\nbackground process whose flock-protected counter increment (and,\npost-script-update, freshness ledger append) runs out-of-band. The\n`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\nthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\nwriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n\nE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\nthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\nper §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\nraces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\nthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n\n### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n\nInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n\n1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n   sub-agent process is spawned.\n2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n   to the parent session.\n\nE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. The SubagentStop\nco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\nthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`) appear somewhere\nin the file — the subagent_stop row coexisting is benign.\n\nE11 (T05.16) will independently assert the paired\n`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` substring + the count-equality\n(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — the\nlatter being a Python-callable predicate that follows the same\ndeferral pattern established in §3 footnote.\n\n## 9. Impacts / dependencies\n\n| Direction | Item | Note |\n|---|---|---|\n| Depends on | T05.01 / D-0082 | OQ-2 resolution — frozen body shape |\n| Depends on | T01.05 (FR-SCH2 validate_eval_id) | accepts literal `E10` |\n| Depends on | T04.02 / T04.03 (Expect.file impl) | satisfied by current `expect.py` |\n| Depends on | T04.04 / T04.05 (Expect.exit_code impl) | satisfied by current `expect.py` |\n| Sibling | T05.07 (E3 body) | first-position SessionStart hook (no-matcher single-substring template — same shape as E10) |\n| Sibling | T05.09 (E5 body) | UserPromptSubmit no-matcher hook (no-matcher single-substring template — same shape as E10) |\n| Sibling | T05.14 (E9 body) | PostToolUse Read async hook (first async hook eval; established the PTY-teardown reap pattern that E10 follows) |\n| Sibling | T05.16 (E11 body) | SubagentStop hook — paired with E10 on sub-agent lifecycle; will assert subagent_stop substring + count-equality predicate |\n| Unblocks | T05.16 (E11 body) | E11 follows the no-matcher async-hook body template established here |\n| Unblocks | T05.18 (CP-P05-T13-T17 checkpoint) | E10 must enumerate + describe; full-run verification follows runner fix |\n\n## 10. The shared input shape (a Task tool invocation)\nfires both SubagentStart (E10's surface) and SubagentStop (E11's\nsurface) on the same prompt. The two evals could in principle have\nbeen folded into one entry with two type-substring assertions, but\nthe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4) keeps them separate for two reasons:\n\n1. **Roadmap-row separation** — E10 maps to R-093, E11 to R-094;\n   merging them would conflate two roadmap items.\n2. **Failure-mode separation** — keeping the two assertions in\n   separate evals lets the test report tell the reviewer\n   independently whether SubagentStart fired but SubagentStop\n   didn't (the dangling-sub-agent case), or vice versa\n   (the orphaned-stop case). Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E11 — the ninth of the\npost-OQ-2 hook-event coverage entries (R-086 … R-098). E11 covers the\n**SubagentStop hook** in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` — whose\ncommand is `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (timeout=1, `async: true`,\nno matcher).\n\nE11 is the **third async hook eval** in the roster — after E9\n(D-0093 / PostToolUse Read async) and E10 (D-0094 / SubagentStart\nasync). Like its async siblings, the matched hook is declared\n`async: true`, so the body relies on PTY teardown reaping the async\nwriter with the binary substring presence as the operationally-\nmeaningful guarantee that the hook fired and flushed before session\nexit.\n\nE11 closes the **sub-agent lifecycle pair** opened by E10. E10\ncovers SubagentStart firing; E11 covers the matched SubagentStop\nfiring with `event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`\nsymmetry called out as a secondary assertion in D-0082 §4 (deferred —\nsee §3 footnote). The two evals are designed as sibling pins on the\nsub-agent spawn/return cycle and share the input shape (spawn a\nsub-agent via the Task tool) while asserting different hook-event\nsubstrings on opposite edges of the lifecycle.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- spawn a fresh claude session via the PTY harness, inject a prompt\n  that invokes a sub-agent via the Task tool (`subagent_type='Explore'`\n  per D-0082 §4 \"e.g., Explore or Plan\" and the E10 D-0094 §\"Why\n  `subagent_type='Explore'`\" rationale — read-only, lightweight,\n  deterministic on a clean HOME), allow the sub-agent to complete and\n  return control to the parent, then exit cleanly so the PTY teardown\n  reaps both the sub-agent and the async SubagentStop hook writer;\n- assert the hook's observable side-effects (freshness ledger present\n  + `subagent_stop` event row);\n- exit cleanly so the `exit_code.equals(0)` assertion can pin a clean\n  `/quit` — and so the PTY teardown reaps any in-flight async writer\n  (sibling pattern to E9 and E10);\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the Task / Agent tool is\n  a built-in Claude Code tool, not an MCP tool, so E11 runs on every\n  host and under `--no-mcp`.\n\n## 2. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStop block:\n\n```jsonc\n\"SubagentStop\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-stop.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 1,\n        \"async\": true }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStop has no\nmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent return fires the same single\nhook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched, E5\nUserPromptSubmit-unmatched, and E10 SubagentStart-unmatched shapes\n(single-substring assertion, no matcher pin needed).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E11 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\nE11's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\nbranch:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart / SubagentStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` | proves the `subagent_stop`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\nthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\nJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_stop` substring pins that\nthe SubagentStop hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\nbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\nshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n\nThe single-substring shape (`subagent_stop`) differs from the\ntwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\nmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStop hook has **no\nmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\nto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` is therefore\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\nneeded (no sibling SubagentStop branches exist).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 row E11 specification phrases the secondary assertion as\nthe start/stop **symmetry** predicate\n`jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=subagent_start) ==\njsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=subagent_stop)`. This\npredicate requires:\n\n1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row twice (or once with\n   two filter accumulators);\n2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"` and rows where\n   `type == \"subagent_stop\"`;\n3. Counting each filtered set and asserting equality.\n\nNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n(`expect.py:269-369`); equality comparison across two filtered counts\nrequires a second-order callable. Following the same precedent as\nE3-E10 sibling deferrals (event_count, per-prompt count\ndiscrimination, event_count >= 1), T05.16 lands the OQ-2 body shape\nwith the binary substring assertion as a proxy: the substring is\npresent iff at least one row carries `\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"`. On a\nsingle Task spawn (the body's input shape), `event_count(subagent_stop)\n== event_count(subagent_start) == 1` is the only outcome where\nsymmetry holds AND substring is present, so the proxy is sufficient\nfor the operationally-meaningful boundary at this spawn count.\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E11 entry that\npreviously carried only stale scaffolding metadata (a placeholder\ntitle from the pre-OQ-2 numbering and no body). T05.16 replaces the\nscaffolding with the frozen body. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n  SubagentStop fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n- The Task tool invocation triggers exactly one SubagentStart event\n  (on spawn) followed by exactly one SubagentStop event (on return)\n  per the single Task prompt; the async SubagentStop hook emits one\n  `subagent_stop`-typed counter decrement (post-script-update, one\n  JSONL row with `\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"`).\n- The chosen sub-agent task (glob `*.md` under cwd) is read-only,\n  deterministic, and bounded — Explore will enumerate the per-eval\n  HOME's markdown files (typically zero on a clean ephemeral HOME)\n  and report the count, then return. No external network calls; no\n  shared-state dependencies.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after the async writer reaps.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n  `session_id`, agent-type / agent-instance fields on the\n  (post-update) JSONL row are not asserted against.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E11` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion.\n\n## 6. The hook writer is a detached background process whose\nflock-protected counter decrement (and, post-script-update, freshness\nledger append) runs out-of-band. The `/quit` teardown reaps the async\nwriter via standard PTY exit-on-EOF: the shell process tree under the\nPTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async writer either completes before\nreceiving (most common case — flock + decrement is microseconds) or\nis killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n\nE11 inherits the E9 / E10 posture: it does NOT assert a specific\nbound on the flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that —\ndeferred per §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\nraces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\nthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n\nThe SubagentStop async window has slightly more headroom than\nSubagentStart's because the sub-agent's return-to-parent and the\nparent's wait at the next prompt give the async writer additional\nmicroseconds before the `/quit` teardown — but the eval body does not\nrely on this margin; the same flock+decrement timing reasoning\nestablished for the SubagentStart sibling carries through unchanged.\n\n### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n\nInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n\n1. **SubagentStart** — async, fires when the sub-agent process is\n   spawned. Covered by E10 (D-0094).\n2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n3. **SubagentStop** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n   sub-agent returns control to the parent session.\n\nE11 asserts **only** the SubagentStop substring. The SubagentStart\nco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\nthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"`) appear somewhere\nin the file — the `subagent_start` row coexisting is benign.\n\nE10 (T05.15) independently asserts the paired `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`\nsubstring. Together, E10 and E11 form a lifecycle pair on the\nsub-agent spawn/return cycle. The OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E11 names a\nstart/stop symmetry predicate\n(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — a\nPython-callable predicate that follows the same deferral pattern\nestablished in §3 footnote.\n\nThe Explore sub-agent's own internal PostToolUse Read co-fires (one\nper Read call inside Explore) emit `\"type\":\"post_read\"` rows to the\nsame `logs/freshness.jsonl` ledger. These coexist with the\n`subagent_stop` row and are harmless to the substring assertion (E9\ncovers the post_read surface independently in D-0093).\n\n## 9. Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E11 — the ninth of the\npost-OQ-2 hook-event coverage entries (R-086 … R-098). E11 covers the\n**SubagentStop hook** in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` — whose\ncommand is `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (timeout=1, `async: true`,\nno matcher).\n\nE11 is the **third async hook eval** in the roster — after E9\n(D-0093 / PostToolUse Read async) and E10 (D-0094 / SubagentStart\nasync). Like its async siblings, the matched hook is declared\n`async: true`, so the body relies on PTY teardown reaping the async\nwriter with the binary substring presence as the operationally-\nmeaningful guarantee that the hook fired and flushed before session\nexit.\n\nE11 closes the **sub-agent lifecycle pair** opened by E10. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStop block:\n\n```jsonc\n\"SubagentStop\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-stop.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 1,\n        \"async\": true }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStop has no\nmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent return fires the same single\nhook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched, E5\nUserPromptSubmit-unmatched, and E10 SubagentStart-unmatched shapes\n(single-substring assertion, no matcher pin needed).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E11 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\nE11's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\nbranch:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart / SubagentStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` | proves the `subagent_stop`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\nthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\nJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_stop` substring pins that\nthe SubagentStop hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\nbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\nshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n\nThe single-substring shape (`subagent_stop`) differs from the\ntwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\nmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStop hook has **no\nmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\nto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` is therefore\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\nneeded (no sibling SubagentStop branches exist).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 row E11 specification phrases the secondary assertion as\nthe start/stop **symmetry** predicate\n`jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=subagent_start) ==\njsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=subagent_stop)`. This\npredicate requires:\n\n1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row twice (or once with\n   two filter accumulators);\n2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"` and rows where\n   `type == \"subagent_stop\"`;\n3. Counting each filtered set and asserting equality.\n\nNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n(`expect.py:269-369`); equality comparison across two filtered counts\nrequires a second-order callable. Following the same precedent as\nE3-E10 sibling deferrals (event_count, per-prompt count\ndiscrimination, event_count >= 1), T05.16 lands the OQ-2 body shape\nwith the binary substring assertion as a proxy: the substring is\npresent iff at least one row carries `\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"`. On a\nsingle Task spawn (the body's input shape), `event_count(subagent_stop)\n== event_count(subagent_start) == 1` is the only outcome where\nsymmetry holds AND substring is present, so the proxy is sufficient\nfor the operationally-meaningful boundary at this spawn count.\n\n## 3. This predicate requires a Python callable\nfilter (`expect.py:269-369`) and a second-order equality across two\nfiltered counts; it is not expressible in declarative YAML. Following\nthe same precedent as E3-E10 sibling deferrals, T05.16 lands the OQ-2\nbody shape with the binary substring assertion as the operational\nproxy. The substring is present iff at least one row carries\n`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"`; on the single-spawn input shape, the\nequality predicate's only satisfying outcome (= 1, = 1) is exactly the\nsubstring-present case, so the proxy preserves the boundary for this\nbody. The strict declarative form remains deferred until either (a)\nthe YAML callback escape hatch (D-4) is exercised for the start/stop\npair, or (b) a future schema bump adds a declarative\n`jsonl: { event_count_equals_event_count: {...} }` shorthand. Neither\nis in scope for T05.16.\n\nNote: invoking the Task tool fires the SubagentStart hook before the\nsub-agent begins and the SubagentStop hook after the sub-agent\nreturns. E11 only asserts the `subagent_stop` substring; E10 (T05.15)\nindependently asserts the paired `subagent_start` substring. Both\nsubstrings coexist in `logs/freshness.jsonl` (post-script-update);\nthe E11 assertion uses `Expect.file.contains` which only requires the\n`subagent_stop` substring to appear somewhere, so the SubagentStart\nco-fire is harmless from E11's perspective.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E11` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E11` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17 evals\nunder suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n  SubagentStop fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n- The Task tool invocation triggers exactly one SubagentStart event\n  (on spawn) followed by exactly one SubagentStop event (on return)\n  per the single Task prompt; the async SubagentStop hook emits one\n  `subagent_stop`-typed counter decrement (post-script-update, one\n  JSONL row with `\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"`).\n- The chosen sub-agent task (glob `*.md` under cwd) is read-only,\n  deterministic, and bounded — Explore will enumerate the per-eval\n  HOME's markdown files (typically zero on a clean ephemeral HOME)\n  and report the count, then return. No external network calls; no\n  shared-state dependencies.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after the async writer reaps.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n  `session_id`, agent-type / agent-instance fields on the\n  (post-update) JSONL row are not asserted against.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E11` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion.\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`). The two-element\n  `inputs[]` array is accepted by the open-shape array schema. The\n  `expect_tool_call` field on `inputs[0]` is accepted by the\n  open-shape (mirrors E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 / E9 / E10 usage).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All three\n  primitives used (2×`file`, 1×`exit_code`) are in `PRIMITIVE_NAMES`\n  (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:186-265`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E11 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E11` (no flags) | RUNS | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E11 --no-mcp` | RUNS | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; Task tool is built-in |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E11 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E11 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9 / E10 (which also carry\n`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\ncapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. The script does NOT write to\n`logs/freshness.jsonl` and does NOT emit a JSONL envelope with a\n`type` field.\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.16; it predates the\ndeliverable and mirrors the identical telemetry gaps discovered\nfor `session-init.sh` during T05.07 (D-0087 §8.1),\n`freshness-session-start.sh` during T05.08 (D-0088 §8.1),\n`freshness-user-prompt.sh` during T05.09 (D-0089 §8.1),\n`freshness-pre-edit.sh` during T05.10 / T05.11 / T05.13\n(D-0090/D-0091/D-0092 §8.1), `freshness-post-read.sh` during\nT05.14 (D-0093 §8.1), and `freshness-subagent-start.sh` during\nT05.15 (D-0094 §8.1). E11 stands alone in script terms (no sibling\nshares `freshness-subagent-stop.sh`); the paired\n`freshness-subagent-start.sh` (E10) is structurally analogous but a\ndistinct script, so the script-update follow-up will land two\nupdates (one per script) for the SubagentStart/SubagentStop pair.\n\nAcceptance criteria for T05.16 (manifest body landed, FR-SCH2-valid\nid, OQ-2 body shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence written) are met\nby the describe / list / roundtrip evidence above; the per-task AC\nthat requires `eval run --eval E11` to exit 0 deterministically\ndepends transitively on (a) the runner NameError fix, and (b) the\nfreshness-subagent-stop.sh emit-observables update with the OQ-2\ncontract path + field name.\n\n### 8.2 Async-branch reaping under PTY teardown\n\nBecause the matched hook is `async: true` (same as E9's PostToolUse\nRead and E10's SubagentStart), the Claude Code runtime does NOT\nblock on the hook's exit before returning control to the parent\nsession. The hook writer is a detached background process whose\nflock-protected counter decrement (and, post-script-update, freshness\nledger append) runs out-of-band. The `/quit` teardown reaps the async\nwriter via standard PTY exit-on-EOF: the shell process tree under the\nPTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async writer either completes before\nreceiving (most common case — flock + decrement is microseconds) or\nis killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n\nE11 inherits the E9 / E10 posture: it does NOT assert a specific\nbound on the flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that —\ndeferred per §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\nraces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\nthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n\nThe SubagentStop async window has slightly more headroom than\nSubagentStart's because the sub-agent's return-to-parent and the\nparent's wait at the next prompt give the async writer additional\nmicroseconds before the `/quit` teardown — but the eval body does not\nrely on this margin; the same flock+decrement timing reasoning\nestablished for the SubagentStart sibling carries through unchanged.\n\n### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n\nInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n\n1. **SubagentStart** — async, fires when the sub-agent process is\n   spawned. Covered by E10 (D-0094).\n2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n3. **SubagentStop** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n   sub-agent returns control to the parent session.\n\nE11 asserts **only** the SubagentStop substring. The SubagentStart\nco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\nthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"`) appear somewhere\nin the file — the `subagent_start` row coexisting is benign.\n\nE10 (T05.15) independently asserts the paired `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`\nsubstring. Together, E10 and E11 form a lifecycle pair on the\nsub-agent spawn/return cycle. The OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E11 names a\nstart/stop symmetry predicate\n(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — a\nPython-callable predicate that follows the same deferral pattern\nestablished in §3 footnote.\n\nThe Explore sub-agent's own internal PostToolUse Read co-fires (one\nper Read call inside Explore) emit `\"type\":\"post_read\"` rows to the\nsame `logs/freshness.jsonl` ledger. These coexist with the\n`subagent_stop` row and are harmless to the substring assertion (E9\ncovers the post_read surface independently in D-0093).\n\n## 9. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01JrFomPjYPi84y9B6NqgYT4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_016nxXL9wwowVMwBUQap6VYs","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0095/notes.md","content":"# D-0095 — Notes / Design Rationale\n\n## Why a Task-tool invocation (not a /agents prompt or other surface)\n\nThe SubagentStop hook fires when a sub-agent process returns control\nto the parent Claude Code session. The canonical user-facing path to\nspawn (and therefore eventually stop) a sub-agent is the **Task tool**\n(the Agent tool's CLI alias), which accepts a `subagent_type`\nparameter naming the agent persona (e.g., `Explore`, `Plan`,\n`general-purpose`). Invoking the Task tool is the most reliable,\ndeterministic way to trigger the SubagentStop event from a single\nuser prompt without depending on session-context flags or in-band\nslash commands — the SubagentStop is the natural counterpart to the\nSubagentStart that Task triggers.\n\nAlternative surfaces considered and rejected (mirroring the E10\nsibling rationale in D-0094, plus E11-specific considerations):\n\n- **`/agents <name>` slash command** — exists in some IronClaude\n  builds but not in the v1 baseline; would require feature-flag\n  gating in the eval body and degrade portability across host\n  Claude Code revisions.\n- **Implicit sub-agent invocation** — depends on the parent agent's\n  persona-routing heuristics; not deterministic across runs.\n- **Multi-step orchestration** — introduces superclaude-specific\n  routing logic into a hook-coverage eval; out of scope.\n- **Aborting a long-running sub-agent** to force SubagentStop on a\n  separate path (e.g., a hung Plan that hits the timeout) — adds\n  non-determinism (timeout race) and reframes the assertion from \"the\n  sub-agent returned cleanly and SubagentStop fired\" to \"the sub-agent\n  was killed and SubagentStop fired in the cleanup path\"; the OQ-2\n  body shape (D-0082 §4 row E11) is satisfied by the normal-return\n  path, so the abort surface is not exercised here.\n\nThe explicit Task-tool invocation with `subagent_type='Explore'`\nfollows the precedent of E10 — and of E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 / E9 more\nbroadly — those evals all name the tool to be invoked explicitly via\nthe prompt + the `expect_tool_call` field on the input row.\n\n## Why `subagent_type='Explore'` (not 'Plan' or 'general-purpose')\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E11 inherits the row E10 example sub-agent type\n(`Explore` or `Plan`). T05.16 picks `Explore` for the same three\nreasons documented in D-0094 §\"Why `subagent_type='Explore'`\":\n\n1. **Read-only by design** — Explore is restricted to Glob, Grep,\n   Read; it cannot Edit / Write / call MCP tools. This keeps the\n   eval body free of unintended PreToolUse co-fires from the\n   sub-agent's own tool calls.\n2. **Lightweight** — Explore's typical work (glob + a few reads) is\n   bounded in time and resource footprint; the 60-second timeout\n   leaves comfortable headroom on slow hosts AND ensures the\n   sub-agent returns (firing SubagentStop) well before the parent's\n   PTY timeout.\n3. **Deterministic on a clean HOME** — the chosen task (\"find files\n   matching `*.md`\") returns zero results on a freshly-isolated\n   per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2). The numeric count of zero is reported\n   back to the parent session, but E11 does not assert on the count\n   — only on the SubagentStop hook firing.\n\nAdditionally for E11 specifically: a sub-agent that does **not**\nreturn cleanly (e.g., one that goes into a recovery branch or\nexception path) might fire SubagentStop on a different code path —\nthe OQ-2 D-0082 row E11 frozen body shape asserts on the\nnormal-return surface, so Explore's clean glob-and-report exit is the\nright shape. `Plan` was again rejected because its potential to spawn\nits own tool calls could create additional hook-event noise that\nmasks regressions where the SubagentStop hook fails to fire (the\nledger might still contain other events from Plan's tool calls,\nfalsely suggesting the assertion's substring is present — except in\nE11's case the substring `subagent_stop` is uniquely produced by the\nSubagentStop hook, so this masking concern is less acute than E10's;\nthe read-only/lightweight/deterministic considerations dominate the\nchoice).\n\n## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n\nThe Task / Agent tool is a **built-in Claude Code tool**, not an MCP\ntool. It is always available regardless of MCP server connectivity.\nPer D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E11's row lists no capability\ntag. This matches sibling E10 (Task / Agent tool) and the broader\nE3-E7 / E9 hook-lifecycle group (no MCP) — and differs from E1,\nE2.1-3, and E8 (which require specific MCP servers).\n\nThe practical implication: E11 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\nmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only\nway E11 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag).\n\n## Why a single-substring assertion (not the two-substring pattern)\n\nE6 / E7 / E8 use a two-substring assertion (`\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` +\n`\"matcher\":\"<branch>\"`) because they cover three branches of the\nsame matcher group (`Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*`) and need to\ndiscriminate which branch fired. The SubagentStop hook in\nhooks.json has **no matcher field** — every sub-agent return fires\nthe same single hook. The `subagent_stop` type label alone is\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no additional matcher pin is\nneeded (and there are no sibling SubagentStop branches to\ndiscriminate against).\n\nThis mirrors E3 (SessionStart-unmatched), E5 (UserPromptSubmit-\nunmatched), and E10 (SubagentStart-unmatched), which all use\nsingle-substring assertions for the same reason.\n\n## Async-branch nuance — why `/quit` is necessary (inherited from E9 / E10)\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E11 inputs say \"spawn session, invoke\nsub-agent as in E10, allow completion, wait for stop hook flush\"\nwithout prescribing a specific wait mechanism for the async hook\nflush. T05.16 implements the wait via the `/quit` clean-exit prompt\n— identical posture to T05.14 (E9) and T05.15 (E10):\n\n- The PTY harness blocks on EOF after `/quit`;\n- PTY teardown reaps any pending async writer processes — either\n  by letting them complete naturally (the expected case for a\n  fast flock+decrement) or by SIGHUP'ing them mid-flight (cleanup\n  path on a slow host);\n- By the time the harness reads `logs/freshness.jsonl` for the\n  Expect.file assertions, the async writer has either:\n  - completed → row present → assertion passes (expected);\n  - been killed mid-flight → row absent → assertion fails\n    (regression surface).\n\nThe `/quit` pattern also satisfies the `exit_code.equals(0)`\nassertion — without `/quit`, the PTY would timeout-kill the session\nand yield a non-zero exit code. This is the sibling pattern of E3-E10\n(all use `/quit` as the final input for the same reason).\n\nFor SubagentStop specifically, there is an additional bonus: the\nsub-agent returns control to the parent before `/quit` is issued, so\nthe SubagentStop hook fires earlier in the prompt sequence than the\nparent's own teardown. The async writer has the gap between\n\"sub-agent returned\" and \"parent processes `/quit`\" to flush — which\nis typically tens of milliseconds on the lightweight Explore task,\nwell in excess of the flock+decrement microseconds. This gives E11\nslightly more flush headroom than E10 (where SubagentStart fires at\nthe parent's busiest moment — the Task tool round-trip itself), but\nthe body does not rely on this margin; the binary substring assertion\nremains the same.\n\nA `sleep` prompt was considered as an alternative wait mechanism but\nrejected for the same reasons documented in D-0093 / D-0094: Claude\nCode does not expose a deterministic sleep tool to the agent; the\nteardown-based wait is already deterministic and matches the pattern\nused by every other hook-lifecycle eval. Adding a sleep would create\na divergent pattern and weaken sibling-trio symmetry.\n\n## Why the Explore sub-agent's own tool calls don't pollute the assertion\n\nThe Explore sub-agent runs under its own process tree, but the hooks\nconfigured under `~/.claude/settings.json` (which point to\n`~/.claude/hooks/*.sh` deployed by `install_hooks`) apply to all tool\ncalls under the same Claude Code session — parent and sub-agent\nalike. So Explore's Glob / Read calls will fire:\n\n- PreToolUse hooks (none — Glob and Read aren't in the\n  `Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*` matcher group);\n- PostToolUse hooks (Read fires the PostToolUse Read async hook\n  covered by E9, emitting `\"type\":\"post_read\"` rows).\n\nThe post_read co-fires from Explore's Read calls coexist in\n`logs/freshness.jsonl` with the SubagentStop row from E11's surface.\nThe E11 assertion uses `Expect.file.contains` which only requires\nthe `subagent_stop` substring to appear somewhere in the file — the\npost_read rows are harmless. Symmetrically, the SubagentStart row\nthat E10 asserts on also coexists in the ledger; E11's assertion\nignores it.\n\nIf a future change introduces a \"filter ledger rows by hook event\"\npredicate (D-4 callback escape hatch), E11's assertion could be\nnarrowed to \"exactly one subagent_stop row, regardless of co-fires\"\n— and the start/stop symmetry predicate could be expressed natively\n— but the current binary substring presence is sufficient for the\nOQ-2 minimum AC.\n\n## Telemetry gap inheritance\n\nThe `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` script telemetry gap (writes to\n`state/bg-agents/<sid>.txt` as a bare integer counter — decremented\nand floored at 0; not `logs/freshness.jsonl`; no `type` field on its\ncurrent output) applies only to E11 — no sibling eval shares this\nscript. The OQ-2 frozen body lands verbatim per the established\nT05.07..T05.15 posture: the script update is a single-script change\nthat unblocks E11 specifically.\n\nUnlike E6/E7/E8 (which share `freshness-pre-edit.sh` and so were\nunblocked by a single script update), there is no batching opportunity\nhere for E11 — E11's hook-script update is independent. E10 (T05.15)\nhad an analogous independent update for `freshness-subagent-start.sh`,\nwhich means the SubagentStart/SubagentStop pair represents two\ndistinct (but structurally identical) follow-up tasks. The same\ntemplate (path migration to `logs/freshness.jsonl` + field-name\nnormalization to `type`) applies to both scripts, so the follow-up\ntasks can reuse the patterns established for the other freshness\nhook scripts.\n\nA side-effect of the pair-separated script architecture: a future\nscript-update change can land both `freshness-subagent-start.sh`\nand `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` in a single commit (they share the\nsame emit-observables template + paired roadmap rows R-093/R-094),\nwhich would be the natural way to ship the SubagentStart/SubagentStop\nJSONL contract together. T05.16 does not own that change; it merely\nunblocks the manifest body for the SubagentStop side.\n\n## Sibling-trio comparison (cross-event surface)\n\nThe nine post-OQ-2 hook-lifecycle evals share a common structural\ntemplate (single-prompt or seeded fire + `/quit` exit; T05.16 closes the loop opened by T05.15. The shared input\nshape (a Task tool invocation) fires both SubagentStart (E10's\nsurface) and SubagentStop (E11's surface) on the same prompt. The\ntwo evals could in principle have been folded into one entry with two\ntype-substring assertions, but the OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4) keeps\nthem separate for two reasons restated here for completeness:\n\n1. **Roadmap-row separation** — E10 maps to R-093, E11 to R-094;\n   merging them would conflate two roadmap items.\n2. **Failure-mode separation** — keeping the two assertions in\n   separate evals lets the test report tell the reviewer\n   independently whether SubagentStart fired but SubagentStop\n   didn't (the dangling-sub-agent case), or vice versa\n   (the orphaned-stop case). A merged eval would mask which side\n   of the pair regressed.\n\nThe start/stop symmetry predicate\n(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) named\nin D-0082 §4 row E11 as a secondary assertion is the cross-pair\ninvariant that would normally live in a merged eval; it is deferred\nper §3 footnote in D-0095 spec.md until either D-4's callback escape\nhatch is exercised or a future schema bump adds a declarative\nshorthand.\n\nThe pair's separation also makes the script-update follow-up natural\nto scope: one diff for `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (unblocking E10),\none diff for `freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (unblocking E11) —\nshippable independently or jointly (per \"Telemetry gap inheritance\"\nabove).
